- `(hr : 0 < r)` (theorem): The statement holds for r ≤ 0 too. If r < 0, closedBall c r = ∅ (Metric.closedBall_eq_empty : closedBall x ε = ∅ ↔ ε < 0), so hx is contradictory. If r = 0, closedBall c 0 = {c} (Metric.closedBall_zero), so x = y = c; the left side is ‖f c - f c‖ = 0 and the right side is card ι * (M / 0) * ‖c - c‖ = card ι * 0 * 0 = 0, so 0 ≤ 0 holds. Proof: split on `r ≤ 0` and handle that branch as above. In the remaining branch `0 < r` holds, so the existing proof applies unchanged.

### [`EulerPacketTerminalDatum.forwardInitializedPacket_normalized_bound`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/PacketForwardInitializedCorrectionData.lean#L76-L84) — `lean-pool-0242`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0242.lean`

- `(hα : 0 < α)` (variable): `hα` follows from `hgrowth : timeProfileChange S.growth hTime = α • L.g` plus positivity built into the structures. `S : Scales (Icc 0 M.T)` has the field `growth_pos : ∀ t, 0 < growth t` (PacketTimeProfiles.lean). `timeProfileChange_pos` (PacketBudgetTimeChange.lean) carries this positivity over to `timeProfileChange S.growth hTime t`. `L : EulerTransversePacketForward.Budget D (Fin 4) 6` has the field `positive : ∀ t, 0 < L.g t` (TransversePacketForwardBudget.lean). `D.T_pos : 0 < D.T` (TransversePacketData.lean), so the interval `Icc 0 D.T` contains t₀ := ⟨0, le_rfl, D.T_pos.le⟩. Evaluating `hgrowth` at t₀ gives 0 < (α • L.g) t₀ = α * L.g t₀ with L.g t₀ > 0, hence 0 < α (if α ≤ 0 the product would be ≤ 0). There are no degenerate cases: the interval is never empty and both profiles are strictly positive. Repair: drop the binder with `omit hα in` and start the proof with `have hα : 0 < α := by have h := timeProfileChange_pos S.growth S.growth_pos hTime t₀; rw [hgrowth] at h; have := L.positive t₀; simp only [ContinuousMap.smul_apply, smul_eq_mul] at h; nlinarith`. The term proof then continues unchanged, passing `hα` to `forwardPacket_normalized_bound` and `forwardInitialized_primary_budget`. This repair was not compiled; the exact simp lemma for applying `α • L.g` pointwise may need adjusting.

### [`LeanPool.Besicovitch.sigma_mul_radius_div_two_le_diam_localAttachmentComponent`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Besicovitch/Rectifiability/ComponentDiameter.lean#L38-L191) — `lean-pool-0244`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0244.lean`

- `(hsigma_one : sigma < 1)` (theorem): The proof uses it only to get `hR_rho : sigma * rho / 2 < rho` by nlinarith. `hannulus` already implies this. It gives a w with `dist w z < rho` (w ∈ ball z rho) and `¬ dist w z < sigma * rho / 2` (w ∉ ball z (sigma*rho/2)), so sigma*rho/2 ≤ dist w z < rho. If sigma*rho/2 ≥ rho the annulus would be empty. So obtain w from hannulus first and prove hR_rho by linarith; the rest of the proof is unchanged.
- `(hsigma : 0 ≤ sigma)` (theorem): Split on the sign of sigma. If sigma < 0, then since 0 < rho we have sigma * rho / 2 < 0 ≤ Metric.diam _ (Metric.diam_nonneg), so the conclusion holds trivially. If 0 ≤ sigma, the existing proof applies verbatim: it uses hsigma only for `hR_nonneg` (positivity) and as the `hβ` argument of `uniformDensitySet_ball_measure_gt`. It can be deleted independently of, or together with, hsigma_one.

### [`EulerTransversePacketProvider.Forcing.velocityPath_reflection_neg`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/TransversePacketParity.lean#L374-L380) — `lean-pool-0247`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0247.lean`

- `(hM : ∀ t x, D.M.field t (-x) = D.M.field t x)` (variable): The proof uses hM only in `D.frameDerivative_even hF hM`, i.e. to get that the frame derivative `D.frameDerivative.field t` is even in x (the hypothesis `hE₁` of `EulerSourceCylinderParity.velocity_reflection_neg`). This follows from hF alone. The structure `Data` contains `frame_time` (F₁ is the time derivative of F within Icc 0 T), and `Data.frame_derivative` gives `HasDerivWithinAt (fun s => extendPath D.T D.T_pos.le D.frame.field s x) (extendPath D.T D.T_pos.le D.frameDerivative.field t x) (Icc 0 D.T) t`. Since `extendPath T hT f s = f (projIcc 0 T hT s)`, `D.frame_even hF` makes the functions for -x and x equal (funext). Derivatives within Icc 0 D.T are unique: Mathlib's `uniqueDiffOn_Icc D.T_pos` (D.T_pos : 0 < D.T is a field of Data, so the interval is never a single point) together with `UniqueDiffWithinAt.eq_deriv`. So the derivative values agree, and `projIcc_of_mem` turns this into `D.frameDerivative.field t (-x) = D.frameDerivative.field t x` for t : Icc 0 D.T. That term replaces `D.frameDerivative_even hF hM`, and the rest of the proof is unchanged. hM appears nowhere in the statement, so deleting it (dropping it from the `include`) leaves the statement well-formed and true.

### [`CKN.norm_sub_integralAverage_le_volumeAverage_integral_norm_fderiv_mul_norm_sub_along_segment`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Foundation/Sobolev/Poincare/Lp.lean#L159-L202) — `lean-pool-0254`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0254.lean`

- `[MeasureTheory.IsFiniteMeasure (volumeMeasureOn U)]` (theorem): `volumeMeasureOn U` is an abbrev for `volumeOn U`, which is `MeasureTheory.volume.restrict U` (RestrictedVolume.lean). The hypothesis `hvol : 0 < (volume U).toReal` gives `volume U < ∞` via `ENNReal.toReal_pos_iff : 0 < a.toReal ↔ 0 < a ∧ a < ∞`. Mathlib's `MeasureTheory.isFiniteMeasure_restrict : IsFiniteMeasure (μ.restrict s) ↔ μ s ≠ ∞` then gives the instance. So the proof can start with `haveI : IsFiniteMeasure (volumeMeasureOn U) := isFiniteMeasure_restrict.2 (ENNReal.toReal_pos_iff.1 hvol).2.ne`, and the rest of the proof is unchanged (`hconstInt` and the call to `norm_sub_integralAverage_le_volumeAverage_integral_norm_sub` use the local instance). No degenerate case is left: `hvol` rules out both infinite and zero volume.
- `(hu : MeasureTheory.IntegrableOn u U)` (theorem): Write g y := ∫ t in 0..1, ‖fderiv ℝ u (segmentBlend x t y)‖ * ‖x - y‖. `hsegment` says g is integrable on U. The library lemma `norm_sub_le_integral_norm_fderiv_mul_norm_sub_along_segment huDiff x y` gives ‖u x - u y‖ ≤ g y for every y. `huDiff.continuous` makes y ↦ u x - u y continuous, hence AE-strongly measurable. So `Integrable.mono' hsegment (by fun_prop/continuity).aestronglyMeasurable (ae_of_all _ bound)` shows that y ↦ u x - u y is integrable on U. The restricted measure is finite (from the instance, or from `hvol` as in the other entry), so the constant y ↦ u x is integrable on U. Then u = (fun _ => u x) - (fun y => u x - u y) is integrable on U. That recovers `hu`, and the existing proof goes through. Edge cases: if U had infinite measure, `hvol` would fail, so nothing is lost. Either this hypothesis or the IsFiniteMeasure instance can be deleted on its own, and both can be deleted together, since the instance is recovered from `hvol` first.

### [`EGZ.CommonMeasure.final_mass_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ErdosGinzburgZiv/EGZ/Decomposition/CommonMeasure.lean#L109-L119) — `lean-pool-0276`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0276.lean`

- `(hε : 0 < ε)` (theorem): If ε ≤ 0 the conclusion is trivial from hu: mass u P ≥ 0 by WeightedIncidence.mass_nonneg u hu P, so ε/2 * mass u P ≤ 0 (mul_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg), and 0 ≤ mass u Q by mass_nonneg u hu Q. If 0 < ε, the existing proof applies. So the proof splits on the sign of ε. This hypothesis can be deleted together with hR.
- `(hR : 0 < R)` (theorem): If R ≤ 0 (and ε > 0): mass u P ≤ mass w P ≤ R ≤ 0 by CommonMeasure.mass_weight_mono w u hle P and hP. So ε/2 * mass u P ≤ 0 ≤ mass u Q, the last step by WeightedIncidence.mass_nonneg u hu Q. If R > 0, the existing proof applies. This can be deleted together with hε: the case ε ≤ 0 is handled as in that entry.

### [`eigenvector_constant_sign`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/SpectralPositivity/Operator/JentzschProof.lean#L332-L387) — `lean-pool-0280`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0280.lean`

- `(hT_compact : IsCompactOperator T)` (theorem): The proof uses hT_compact only by passing it to `abs_eigenvector_of_top_eigenvector` (JentzschProof.lean line 221). That lemma names the binder `_hT_compact` and its proof (lines 236-284) never uses it. The proof steps are: norm_abs_eq_norm, abs_inner_le_inner_abs (which uses positivity preservation), hlam₀_top, hT_sa.eq_smul_self_of_isLocalExtrOn (which needs only self-adjointness), and computing the Rayleigh quotient. The other lemma called, `ground_state_strictly_positive`, takes no compactness argument. hT_compact is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement. So the theorem holds without it. To prove it, drop the argument from the call, or remove the unused binder from abs_eigenvector_of_top_eigenvector as well (the call needs a compactness proof as long as that binder stays).

### [`QuadraticCarleson.annularQuadraticKernel_correlation_le_ordered`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticFixedHeightAveragingCorrelation.lean#L49-L127) — `lean-pool-0281`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0281.lean`

- `(hD : 0 ≤ D)` (theorem): `hD` follows from `hab` and `hR`. For any point, say 0, we have `0 ≤ ‖a 0‖ ≤ D / R`. Since `0 < R`, `le_div_iff₀ hR` turns this into `0 * R ≤ D`, so `0 ≤ D`. The proof can therefore start with `have hD : 0 ≤ D := by have h := (le_div_iff₀ hR).mp ((norm_nonneg _).trans (hab 0)); simpa using h` and then run unchanged. Inside the proof, `hD` only serves the `positivity` side goals (e.g. `0 ≤ D / R`, `0 ≤ D / S`) and `hP`, and the derived fact covers all of them. No degenerate case causes trouble: `R > 0` is assumed, so the division is not the junk `x / 0`.

### [`AVenhance.Infra.Section3.ChiMKCorrector.chiMK_spaceLap_three_active`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AnomalousDiffusion/AVenhance/Infra/Section3/ChiMKCorrector.lean#L159-L209) — `lean-pool-0282`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0282.lean`

- `(hk : k % 4 = 3)` (theorem): The identity also holds when `k % 4 ≠ 3`, so `hk` can be deleted. `chiMK κ m k t x = (-(corrTime κ m k t)) • uShear β Λ m k x` (ChiMK.lean), and `uShear` (UShear.lean) is `![0, …]` when `k % 4 = 1`, `![-(…cos(2π x 1/ε)), 0]` when `k % 4 = 3`, and `0` otherwise. So whenever `k % 4 ≠ 3`, the coordinate `uShear β Λ m k y 0` is `0` for every `y`, and `fun y => I.chiMK κ m k t y 0` is the constant zero function.

Proof without `hk`: do `by_cases hk : k % 4 = 3`. In the positive case, use the existing proof. In the negative case:
1. Show `(fun y => I.chiMK κ m k t y 0) = fun _ => 0` with `funext y; simp [Ingredients.chiMK, uShear, hk]`, splitting on `k % 4 = 1` if needed. Either way the 0-coordinate is 0.
2. `spaceGrad (fun _ => 0) y i = fderiv ℝ (fun _ => 0) y (basisVec i) = 0` by `fderiv_const`. The proof already does this step via `simp [AVenhance.spaceGrad]` for `hzeroGrad`.
3. Then the inner gradient function is identically 0, the outer one is too, and `spaceLap = 0 + 0 = 0`.
4. The right-hand side is `-(4π²/ε²) * I.chiMK κ m k t x 0 = -(4π²/ε²) * 0 = 0`.

No degenerate case breaks this argument: the right-hand side has the factor `chiMK … x 0 = 0` whatever the value of ε. So the statement stays true and provable without `hk`.

### [`TauCeti.sum_sq_principalCosines_eq_sum_sq_norm_projection`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/AngleGeometry.lean#L747-L767) — `lean-pool-0283`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0283.lean`

- `[U.HasOrthogonalProjection]` (theorem): Implied by the other hypotheses. `[FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E]` (from `variable`) gives `FiniteDimensional 𝕜 U` through the standard submodule instance. Mathlib's `RCLike.properSpace_submodule` (Analysis/RCLike/Lemmas.lean) then gives `ProperSpace U`, so `U` is a `CompleteSpace`, and `HasOrthogonalProjection.ofCompleteSpace` (Projection/Basic.lean) gives `U.HasOrthogonalProjection`. `HasOrthogonalProjection` is a `Prop`-valued class, so by proof irrelevance the synthesized instance is definitionally the same as the binder. The statement (`principalCosines U V`, `projection V`) elaborates the same way, and the proof goes through unchanged, with the lemmas it calls (`singularValues_cosThetaMap_comp_subtype`, `starProjection_eq_self_iff`, ...) getting the synthesized instance. Removing this binder together with `[V.HasOrthogonalProjection]` also works.
- `[V.HasOrthogonalProjection]` (theorem): Same argument as for `U`. `FiniteDimensional 𝕜 V` follows from `[FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E]`, then `RCLike.properSpace_submodule` gives `ProperSpace V`, hence `CompleteSpace V`, and `HasOrthogonalProjection.ofCompleteSpace` gives `V.HasOrthogonalProjection`. The class is a `Prop`, so the synthesized instance agrees with the binder by proof irrelevance. The statement and the proof are unchanged.

### [`Malliavin.memLp_mderiv_cylinder_expGrowth`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Malliavin/Malliavin/CylindricalGrowth.lean#L488-L496) — `lean-pool-0295`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0295.lean`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): `hC` follows from `hfg'`. Take `y = 0`, which exists in `Fin n → ℝ` for every `n`, including `n = 0`. Then `0 ≤ ‖fderiv ℝ f 0‖ ≤ C * Real.exp (c * ‖0‖) = C * Real.exp 0 = C`. In Lean: `have hC : 0 ≤ C := (norm_nonneg _).trans (by simpa using hfg' 0)`. The existing proof then goes through unchanged. This works whether or not `hc` is kept.
- `(hc : 0 ≤ c)` (theorem): The statement still holds when `c < 0`. Set `c' := max c 0 ≥ 0`. Since `‖y‖ ≥ 0`, we have `c * ‖y‖ ≤ c' * ‖y‖`, hence `Real.exp (c * ‖y‖) ≤ Real.exp (c' * ‖y‖)`. Multiplying by `C ≥ 0` (kept, or derived from `hfg'` as for `hC`) gives `hfg'' : ∀ y, ‖fderiv ℝ f y‖ ≤ C * Real.exp (c' * ‖y‖)`. The current proof then runs with `c'` in place of `c`: `memLp_mderiv_cylinder_of_dominated μ hf L (ψ := fun x ↦ C * Real.exp (c' * ((∑ i, ‖L i‖) * ‖x‖))) hfg'' (expGrowth_coord_le hC (le_max_right c 0) L) (integrable_expGrowth_sq μ C c' _)`. Here `integrable_expGrowth_sq` holds for any real `c`. Equivalently, apply the theorem itself with `c'`. The conclusion does not mention `c`, so nothing else changes. `hc` and `hC` can also be removed together.

### [`Malliavin.BrownianIteratedConstruction.wickPurePowerMixedInnerIdentity_of_generated`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Malliavin/Malliavin/BrownianPurePowerDuality.lean#L393-L402) — `lean-pool-0298`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0298.lean`

- `(hsm : ∀ t, StronglyMeasurable (B t))` (theorem): The other hypotheses already imply `hsm`. For each `t`, `coordinate_apply t` together with `funext` gives `B t = ⇑(coordinate t)`. Here `coordinate t : StrongDual ℝ W` is a continuous linear map, so `(coordinate t).continuous` holds. Mathlib's `Continuous.stronglyMeasurable` (Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/Basic.lean:723) then gives `StronglyMeasurable (B t)`. Its instance requirements are met: `[OpensMeasurableSpace W]` follows from `[BorelSpace W]`; `ℝ` is pseudo-metrizable; and `SecondCountableTopologyEither W ℝ` holds because `ℝ` (and also `W`) is second countable. No degenerate case needs separate treatment, since this argument works for every `t`. So `hsm` can be removed from the statement. The proof starts with `have hsm : ∀ t, StronglyMeasurable (B t) := fun t => by rw [show B t = ⇑(coordinate t) from funext (coordinate_apply t)]; exact (coordinate t).continuous.stronglyMeasurable` and then calls the same two lemmas as before. A second route also works: `generated` makes each `B t` measurable (`IsWienerGenerated.measurable`), and `Measurable.stronglyMeasurable` applies because `ℝ` is second countable.

### [`jensen_map_maxIdeal_eq_of_surj_closed`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AndersonConjecture/Jensen/Jensen.lean#L479-L486) — `lean-pool-0305`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0305.lean`

- `(h_closed : ∀ (I : Ideal ↥R), I.FG → ∀ (c : ↥R), (c : T) ∈ Ideal.map R.subtype I → c ∈ I)` (theorem): The proof uses h_closed only for the inclusion `Ideal.map R.subtype (maximalIdeal R) ≤ maximalIdeal T`, through `map_maxIdeal_le_of_closed`. That inclusion already follows from h_surj and the locality of R. Compose `r ↦ Ideal.Quotient.mk (M_T^2) r`, surjective by h_surj, with the quotient map T ⧸ M_T^2 → T ⧸ M_T = ResidueField T, which is surjective because M_T^2 ≤ M_T. This gives a surjective ring hom from R onto the field ResidueField T. Its kernel is `(maximalIdeal T).comap R.subtype`. By `RingHom.ker_isMaximal_of_surjective` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/Maps.lean) that kernel is a maximal ideal of R. Since R is local, `IsLocalRing.eq_maximalIdeal` gives kernel = maximalIdeal R. Hence maximalIdeal R ≤ comap R.subtype M_T, which is equivalent to the `≤` inclusion by `Ideal.map_le_iff_le_comap`. The reverse inclusion in `map_maxIdeal_eq_of_surj_closed` is a Nakayama argument that uses only h_surj, `comap_maxIdeal_le_of_local` and T being Noetherian; none of these involve h_closed. There are no degenerate cases: T is local, so its residue field is a nontrivial field. Removing h_closed is necessary-safe on its own; h_surj cannot also be removed (R = ℂ ⊆ T is a counterexample).

### [`QuadraticCarleson.integral_le_four_mul_succ_of_le_lowDecayMajorant`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/LowOscillatoryMajorant.lean#L307-L344) — `lean-pool-0312`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0312.lean`

- `(hFmeas : AEStronglyMeasurable F (volume.restrict (Icc (z - ℓ) (z + ℓ))ᶜ))` (theorem): Split on `IntegrableOn F (Icc (z-ℓ) (z+ℓ))ᶜ`. If `F` is not integrable there, `integral_undef` makes the left side 0, and `0 ≤ 4*(B+1)*A` holds by `hA` and `B ≥ 0`. If it is integrable, that integrability is exactly the `hFint` the proof builds from `hFmeas`, and the rest of the calc (integral_mono_ae, integral_const_mul, integral_lowDecayMajorant_two_pow_le, hA) goes through unchanged. Deleting this needs `hA` to stay.
- `(hFnonneg : ∀ x ∉ Icc (z - ℓ) (z + ℓ), 0 ≤ F x)` (theorem): `hFnonneg` is used only to show `F` is integrable by domination. Instead, split on integrability. If `F` is not integrable on the complement, the integral is 0 ≤ 4*(B+1)*A by `hA`. If it is integrable, `integral_mono_ae` with `hFbound` needs only the upper bound, and the rest of the calc is unchanged. Deleting this needs `hA` to stay, so it cannot be dropped together with `hA`.
- `(hA : 0 ≤ A)` (theorem): `hA` follows from the other hypotheses. With `ℓ > 0`, take `x = z + 2ℓ`, which lies outside `Icc (z-ℓ) (z+ℓ)`. There `lowDecayMajorant (2^(2B)) ℓ z x = min (1/(2ℓ)) (2^(2B)*ℓ/(4ℓ^2)) > 0`. Then `hFnonneg` and `hFbound` give `0 ≤ F x ≤ A * (positive)`, hence `0 ≤ A` (e.g. `nonneg_of_mul_nonneg_left`, or a short case argument). The original proof then goes through with this derived `hA`. This needs `hFnonneg`, `hFbound` and `hℓ` to stay.
- `(hℓ : 0 < ℓ)` (theorem): `lowDecayMajorant D ℓ z x = min (1/|x-z|) (D*ℓ/|x-z|^2)`, with `D = 2^(2B) > 0`. If `ℓ = 0`, the complement is `{z}ᶜ`, and for `x ≠ z` the majorant is `min (positive) 0 = 0`. So `0 ≤ F x ≤ A*0 = 0`, `F` vanishes on the set, and the integral is 0 ≤ 4*(B+1)*A by `hA`. If `ℓ < 0`, `Icc (z-ℓ) (z+ℓ)` is empty, so the bounds hold at every `x`. For `x ≠ z`, `D*ℓ/|x-z|^2 < 0`, so the majorant is negative and `A*majorant ≤ 0`. At `x = z`, both terms are junk `0`. Hence `F ≡ 0` and the integral is 0 ≤ RHS. If `ℓ > 0`, use the original proof. Deleting this needs `hA` and `hFnonneg` to stay.

### [`NavierStokes.LocalSignedRequest.SlowRegion.chartQ_pos`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/LocalSignedRequest.lean#L2030-L2032) — `lean-pool-0332`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0332.lean`

- `(hx : x.2.1 ∈ U.carrier)` (theorem): `MeanRankUpdate.chartQ coord x` is positive for every `coord` and every `x`, so the membership hypothesis is not needed. By `chartQ_eq_slow` (LocalSignedRequest.lean:1243), `MeanRankUpdate.chartQ coord p = SimilarityCoordinates.coordinateQ coord (p.2.1.1, p.2.1.2)`. The proof term in the theorem also relies on this, since it uses `U.q_mem x.2.1 hx`. In SimilarityCoordinates.lean:135, `coordinateQ a p` is defined as `if hp : 0 < a ∧ a < 1 ∧ 0 < p.1 then Classical.choose (exists_positive_solution ...) else 1`. In the first branch, `Classical.choose_spec` gives `0 < q`, because `exists_positive_solution` states `∃ q, 0 < q ∧ …`. In the junk branch the value is `1 > 0`. There is no other case, so no extra assumption on `coord` or `x` is needed. Proof without `hx`: `unfold MeanRankUpdate.chartQ PhysicalCoordinateBounds.qCoord SimilarityCoordinates.coordinateQ; split_ifs with h; exacts [(Classical.choose_spec (SimilarityCoordinates.exists_positive_solution h.1 h.2.1 h.2.2 _)).1, one_pos]`. Alternatively, rewrite with `chartQ_eq_slow` first and then case-split in the same way. After deleting `hx`, `U` remains an argument that the proof does not use. That is allowed, because only `hx` is being deleted. `U` cannot be deleted on its own, since `hx` mentions `U.carrier`.

### [`NavierStokes.RadialPullback.pullback_contDiff`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/RadialPullback.lean#L1400-L1402) — `lean-pool-0338`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0338.lean`

- `(ha : 0 < a)` (theorem): pullback d a F = F ∘ liftChart (powerChart d a), where powerChart d a R = (positiveRadius (a/4) R) ^ d and positiveRadius ℓ x = ℓ + (x - ℓ) * smoothTransition ((x - ℓ)/ℓ). positiveRadius_contDiff ℓ holds for every ℓ. ha is used only to get positiveRadius (a/4) R ≠ 0, which ContDiff.rpow_const_of_ne needs. Without ha: (1) if a = 0, then (x-0)/0 = 0, so positiveRadius 0 x = x * smoothTransition 0 = 0 (Real.smoothTransition.zero). powerChart d 0 is the constant 0^d, smooth by contDiff_const. (2) if a < 0, put ℓ = a/4 < 0. For x ≥ ℓ, (x-ℓ)/ℓ ≤ 0, so smoothTransition gives 0 (smoothTransition.zero_of_nonpos) and the value is ℓ. For x < ℓ, (x-ℓ) < 0 and smoothTransition ≥ 0 (smoothTransition.nonneg), so the value is ≤ ℓ. Hence positiveRadius ℓ x ≤ ℓ < 0 never vanishes, and ContDiff.rpow_const_of_ne applies. (3) if a > 0, this is the existing proof. Then liftChart_contDiff and hF.comp finish as before. Nothing else in the statement mentions ha.

### [`NavierStokes.EndpointCoordinates.composition_endpoint_smoothAt`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/EndpointCoordinates.lean#L337-L342) — `lean-pool-0344`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0344.lean`

- `(hh : 0 < h)` (theorem): Write a = 2h. The proof uses `hh` in only two ways. It does not need it for the endpoint being in the domain, and it needs it for smoothness only because a library lemma asks for 0 ≤ a.

(1) Membership. `cartesian_endpoint_mem` goes through `endpoint_mem_domain`, which calls `zeroTime_mem_stableTarget`. In that lemma the positivity argument is named `_ha` and is never used. The witness `(endpointRoot a z, z)` works for every a < 1 and z ≠ 0: `endpointRoot_pos`, `endpointRoot_equation` and `endpointRoot_slope` (slope = 1 - a > 0) only need `a < 1`, and that follows from `hh1` alone. So `(1, x) ∈ cartesianDomain h` for every h < 1/2.

(2) Smoothness. `cartesianExtension_smoothAt` reduces to `qExtension_smoothAt`, then to `PositiveRepresentatives.stableInverse_smoothAt (ha : 0 ≤ a) (ha1 : a ≤ 1)`. That lemma uses `ha` only through `stableInverse_forwardMap`, i.e. `forwardMap_injOn_stable` (injectivity of `q ↦ forwardScalar a z q` on `stableSource`). `forwardMap_smooth`, `forwardMap_hasFDerivAt`, `stableSource_open` and `stableTarget_open` take no sign assumption on a.

When a ≤ 0 (h ≤ 0), injectivity still holds. We have `scalarSlope a z q = 1 - z^2 * a * q^(a-1) ≥ 1` for q > 0, because z^2 ≥ 0, -a ≥ 0 and q^(a-1) > 0. This is the derivative of `forwardScalar a z` (`forwardScalar_hasDerivAt`). So `strictMonoOn_of_deriv_pos` gives strict monotonicity on any `Icc q r` with q > 0, exactly as in `forwardScalar_lt_on_stable`, with the bound `slope ≥ 1` replacing `scalarSlope_mono`. Hence `stableInverse (forwardMap a p) = p` on `stableSource a` for every a ≤ 1, and the inverse-function argument in `stableInverse_smoothAt` goes through unchanged.

The remaining steps (`innerExtension_smoothAt` via `qExtension_pos`, composition with `contDiff_profilePoint`, and finally `hf.comp`) do not involve h's sign.

Degenerate case h = 0: forwardScalar 0 z q = q - z^2 and the endpoint root is z^2 > 0, so the argument works. Thus the statement holds without `hh`, as long as `hh1` and `hx` stay. `hx` is genuinely needed: at x 2 = 0 the point leaves the stable target and the extension is discontinuous there. `hh1` is also kept.

### [`QuantumParallelRepetition.unconditionalCanonicalAcceptedCoefficient_sourceScale`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuantumParallelRepetition/Part09.lean#L1438-L1492) — `lean-pool-0384`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0384.lean`

- `(dimension : 0 < d)` (theorem): The theorem also takes `i : Fin d`, and `Fin.pos i : 0 < d` (Init/Data/Fin/Basic.lean) derives the same fact. When d = 0 the type Fin 0 is empty, so the statement holds vacuously. The proof only uses `dimension` to get `(d:ℝ) ≠ 0` and for `positivity`; adding `have dimension := Fin.pos i` at the start leaves the rest of the proof unchanged.
- `(grid : 0 < N)` (theorem): Case N = 0. The lemma `dSVUniformDensityThresholdRaw_norm_sq` (no hypotheses) gives ‖raw‖² = d·0 = 0, so ‖raw‖ = 0. Its inverse is 0⁻¹ = 0, so the left side is 0. On the right, `dSVDensityRationalPhysicalAcceptedRank_gridPrefix` (no hypotheses) rewrites the grid prefix as r/0 = 0, so the right side is √(w·0) = 0. Both sides are 0. Case N > 0: use the original proof. So `grid` is not needed; the proof just splits on whether N = 0.
- `(width : 0 < w)` (theorem): Case w ≤ 0. We have d ≥ 0, so w·d ≤ 0 and `Real.sqrt_eq_zero'` gives √(w·d) = 0; the left side is 0. On the right, unfold `dSVDensityRationalCanonicalAcceptedCoefficient` (= √(w * gridPrefix …)). The rank lemma `dSVDensityRationalPhysicalAcceptedRank_gridPrefix` (no hypotheses) rewrites the grid prefix as r/N, which is ≥ 0 because r ≥ 0 and N ≥ 0 as reals. So w·(r/N) ≤ 0 and the right side is also 0. Case w > 0: use the original proof, which needs `width` only through `..._targetCoefficient_sq`. So `width` can be removed by splitting on the sign of w. Each of the three hypotheses (`dimension`, `grid`, `width`) can be removed on its own, and the same case analyses also work with all of them removed together.

### [`NatOrdinal.exists_lt_forall_add_add_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ConwayRefinement/ConwayRefinement/SetTheory/Ordinal/PairBounds.lean#L206-L237) — `lean-pool-0393`

- `(ht₁ : t₁ ≠ 0)` (theorem): Implied by he₁. `leastTerm_zero : leastTerm (0 : NatOrdinal) = 0` (ConwayRefinement/SetTheory/Ordinal/LeastTerm.lean) and `wpow_ne_zero (x : NatOrdinal) : ω^ x ≠ 0` (CombinatorialGames/NatOrdinal/Pow.lean). If t₁ = 0, then he₁ becomes 0 = ω^ e₁, which contradicts wpow_ne_zero. To fix the proof, first recover the hypothesis with `have ht₁ : t₁ ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; rw [leastTerm_zero] at he₁; exact wpow_ne_zero e₁ he₁.symm`, then run the existing proof unchanged.
- `(ht₂ : t₂ ≠ 0)` (theorem): Same argument, using he₂. If t₂ = 0, then `leastTerm_zero` turns he₂ into 0 = ω^ e₂, which contradicts `wpow_ne_zero e₂`. Recover it with `have ht₂ : t₂ ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; rw [leastTerm_zero] at he₂; exact wpow_ne_zero e₂ he₂.symm` and keep the rest of the proof. Both ht₁ and ht₂ can be deleted together, because each is derived from its own leastTerm equation.

### [`RS.EdgeSubset.isOut_pushData_liftData_succ_closed_at`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/RegtsSevenster/RS/Novel/Skein/ConverseFamily.lean#L868-L926) — `lean-pool-0394`

- `(hEL : (EdgeSubset.mk (liftSubsetClosed (V.dropSubset (cutL n) (cutR n) s) (decide (V.boundaryFlag (cutL n) ∈ s))) hcL : EdgeSubset V).Eulerian)` (theorem): By `RS.Fragment.liftSubsetClosed_dropSubset` (GlueSubsetBij.lean:382), whose inputs are `hij : i ≠ j`, `hclosed` and closure of `s`, we get `liftSubsetClosed (V.dropSubset (cutL n) (cutR n) s) (decide (V.boundaryFlag (cutL n) ∈ s)) = s`. Here we supply `cutL_ne_cutR n` (already used in the proof), `hcl` and `hc`. Because the left side mentions `s`, `subst` cannot be used. Instead prove `∀ t (ht : t = s) hct, (EdgeSubset.mk t hct).Eulerian` by `rintro t rfl hct; exact hE`. This works because by proof irrelevance `EdgeSubset.mk s hct` and `EdgeSubset.mk s hc` are definitionally equal. Applying it to the lifted set, the lemma's equality and `hcL` recovers `hEL`, which is then passed to `isOut_unglue_glueDataClosed` as before. Nothing else in the statement mentions `hEL`. It can be deleted together with hneL.
- `(hneL : Nonempty (EdgeSubset.mk (liftSubsetClosed (V.dropSubset (cutL n) (cutR n) s) (decide (V.boundaryFlag (cutL n) ∈ s))) hcL : EdgeSubset V).CanonData)` (theorem): Same argument as for hEL. `liftSubsetClosed_dropSubset (cutL_ne_cutR n) hcl s hc` shows the lifted set equals `s`. Then `∀ t (ht : t = s) hct, Nonempty (EdgeSubset.mk t hct).CanonData`, proved by `rintro t rfl hct; exact hne` (proof irrelevance makes the two EdgeSubsets definitionally equal), recovers `hneL` from `hne`. The rest of the proof is unchanged, and `hneL` is not mentioned elsewhere in the statement. It can be deleted together with hEL.

### [`LeanStationaryHarmonicMaps.StationaryHarmonicMap.weak_radial_cutoff_integrable_of_W12Loc_contDiffBump`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LeanStationaryHarmonicMaps/StationaryHarmonicMap/RadialIntegrability.lean#L309-L324) — `lean-pool-0396`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0396.lean`

- `(hRout : f.rOut < R0)` (theorem): The proof uses `hRout` only to build an `AdmissibleRadialCutoff` through `ContDiffBump.exists_admissibleRadialCutoff`. The library's own lemma `weak_radial_cutoff_integrable_of_locallyL2` (same file) does not need that structure. It needs: `GradientAEStronglyMeasurableIn Du Ω` and `GradientLocallyL2In Du Ω` (both come from `hW` via `hW.gradient_aestronglyMeasurable` and `hW.gradient_locallyL2`), `hclosedBall_subset`, `ContDiff ℝ 1 phi`, bounds `‖phi t‖ ≤ M0` and `‖deriv phi t‖ ≤ M1` for t in [0, R0], and `0 ≤ R0`. None of these involves where the bump vanishes, so how `rOut` compares with `R0` does not matter. The coefficients `weakRadialMainCoeff n phi x = (n-2)·phi ‖x‖ + ‖x‖·deriv phi ‖x‖` and `weakRadialRhsCoeff phi x = ‖x‖·deriv phi ‖x‖` are bounded on the ball for any C¹ function phi.

Proof without `hRout`, with phi = (f ·):
- Case R0 < 0: `Metric.ball 0 R0` is empty, so both `IntegrableOn` statements hold by `integrableOn_empty`.
- Case 0 ≤ R0: apply `weak_radial_cutoff_integrable_of_locallyL2` with:
  - `f.contDiff` (Mathlib `ContDiffBump.contDiff`, valid at every smoothness order) for `ContDiff ℝ 1`;
  - M0 = 1, using `f.nonneg` and `f.le_one`;
  - M1 = a bound on `deriv f` over the compact set `Icc 0 R0`. This bound exists because `deriv f` is continuous (`ContDiff.continuous_deriv` applied to `f.contDiff`), via `IsCompact.exists_bound_of_continuousOn`.

The degenerate case R0 ≤ 0 is covered by the empty ball (or, at R0 = 0, by the lemma itself). So the statement stays true and provable after deleting `hRout`.


## FLT (Anthropic) (82 flagged)

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_full_eq_adjoin_primes.ModularCurve.W1.roots_prime_at_slot_roots_nodup`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_full_eq_adjoin_primes.lean#L306-L312) — `flt-anthropic-0001`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0001.lean`

- `(hζ : IsPrimitiveRoot (ζ : K) N)` (theorem): The conclusion never mentions N or ζ. It only says that the roots in LaurentSeries K of P := Φ_p(X, twisted j(q^{pe})) have no duplicates. The hypotheses hζ and hpN are only there so that K contains a primitive p-th root of unity, which lets P split explicitly over LaurentSeries K. The statement still holds without them, by base change. Let L be a field extension of K containing a primitive p-th root of unity ζ', e.g. CyclotomicField p K. This exists because char K = 0 (from [Algebra ℚ K]), so (p : K) ≠ 0. Let f = algebraMap K L. The library's coeffMap f : LaurentSeries K →+* LaurentSeries L acts coefficientwise. It commutes with qExpand (the library lemma coeffMap_qExpand). It commutes with qTwist, with u replaced by Units.map f u, because qTwistFun multiplies coefficient k by u^k. It also satisfies coeffMap f ∘ coeffEmb K = coeffEmb L, by uniqueness of ℚ-algebra maps and coeffMap_coeffMap. So P.map (coeffMap f) is the same polynomial built over L. In L, apply the existing lemmas with N := p, ζ := ζ' (so hpN is dvd_refl): roots_prime_at_slot gives p+1 roots and roots_prime_at_slot_nodup says they are distinct. The mapped polynomial is monic of degree p+1 (data.monic, natDegree_eq), so it splits. By Mathlib's Polynomial.nodup_roots_iff_of_splits it is separable. Polynomial.separable_map, using that LaurentSeries K is a field, then makes P separable, and Polynomial.nodup_roots gives P.roots.Nodup. No degenerate case is an issue: K is an arbitrary characteristic-0 field, which is exactly the case the base change handles. The proof needs this rewrite, but every Mathlib lemma it uses was found in the sources.
- `(hpN : p ∣ N)` (theorem): Same argument as for hζ. The conclusion does not involve N or ζ. The base change to L = CyclotomicField p K, applying roots_prime_at_slot and roots_prime_at_slot_nodup there with N := p and a primitive p-th root of unity of L, together with Polynomial.nodup_roots_iff_of_splits, Polynomial.separable_map and Polynomial.nodup_roots, proves the statement without assuming that p divides N.
- `[NeZero N]` (theorem): N enters only through hζ and hpN, and the conclusion does not depend on N. The base-change proof described for hζ uses neither the given N nor the given ζ. It works in CyclotomicField p K with N := p, where NeZero p comes from p being prime. So the statement holds without NeZero N; N = 0, where IsPrimitiveRoot ζ 0 means ζ has infinite order, is no problem.

The same file shows that the abbrev does not take the `Fact`: `coe_jChartFin` is stated with `omit [Fact (j ≠ 0)] in` and still mentions `jChartFin R F j : chartAlgFin R F j`, which would fail to elaborate otherwise. The theorem is also true without the assumption, since the argument is pure integrality. The current proof only uses the `Fact`s to call `chartAlgFin_le_of_mem` (lines 77-94 of the same file), which takes them as binders, but that proof never uses them. It uses only `adjoin_le_chartAlg`, `Algebra.adjoin_le`, `IsIntegral.map_of_comp_eq` and `isIntegral_trans`, none of which need nonzeroness. Inlining that proof twice, or dropping the `Fact` binders from `chartAlgFin_le_of_mem` as well, gives the antisymmetry without this hypothesis. The inclusion proof in `chartAlgFin_le_of_mem` (adjoin ≤ chartAlg, then `isIntegral_trans` through the integral extension `Algebra.adjoin R {b} → chartAlgFin R F b`) never uses `b ≠ 0`. ### [`P2MW.S_exists_isIdempotentElem_mul_iterate_eq_zero_sum_iterate_eq_one_of_not_isField.C2idemCore.IdempotentOrbit.exists_idempotent_orbit_or_isField`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_exists_isIdempotentElem_mul_iterate_eq_zero_sum_iterate_eq_one_of_not_isField.lean#L172-L278) — `flt-anthropic-0014`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0014.lean`

- `[Finite ι]` (theorem): The other hypotheses force ι to be finite. Empty ι: R is trivial (φ.toEquiv.subsingleton) and the first disjunct holds with e = 0, as in the existing proof. Otherwise pick j₀. Let t = conj φ s; t^n = id with n ≥ 1, so t is a ring automorphism of P = Π i, F i. Each F i is a field, so the idempotents of P are exactly the {0,1}-valued functions, i.e. indicators of arbitrary subsets of ι, even when ι is infinite. Both t and t⁻¹ preserve the order e ≤ e' ↔ e*e' = e, so t sends the minimal nonzero idempotents ind {i} to each other: t (ind {i}) = ind {ψ i} for a permutation ψ. From t^n = id and injectivity of ind on singletons, ψ^[n] = id, so the orbit O = (range n).image (ψ^[·] j₀) is a finite set mapped onto itself by ψ. Since ind O is a finite sum of singleton indicators, t (ind O) = ind (ψ '' O) = ind O. So a = φ.symm (ind O) is an idempotent fixed by s. By hfix, a = f x; since f is injective, x is an idempotent of the field k, hence 0 or 1, so ind O is 0 or 1. As j₀ ∈ O, ind O ≠ 0, so ind O = 1, which gives O = univ. Hence ι is finite. Then Fintype.ofFinite supplies the [Fintype ι] that the helper exists_indexMap_of_iterate_eq needs, and the existing proof applies unchanged. The statement is therefore still true and provable without [Finite ι].

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_relfinrank_qExpFunctionFieldC_gammaH_gammaH_inf_gamma0_mul_eq_add_one.CohCarrier.gammaH_inf_gamma0_mul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_relfinrank_qExpFunctionFieldC_gammaH_gammaH_inf_gamma0_mul_eq_add_one.lean#L104-L128) — `flt-anthropic-0049`

- `[NeZero N]` (theorem): The statement does not need it to elaborate. `CohCarrier.GammaH M H := (H.comap (gamma0Units M)).map (Gamma0 M).subtype` is defined for any `M : ℕ` with no NeZero assumption (Definitions/Def_CohCarrier_Level.lean), and so are `gamma0Units` and `Gamma0_d_mul_a`. `ZMod.unitsMap hm := Units.map (castHom hm _)` needs only divisibility. The proof never uses it: it only uses `map_intCast`, `mem_GammaH_iff`, `Subgroup.mem_comap`, `Gamma0_mem` and `map_zero`, plus `simp` with `gamma0Units`, `Gamma0Map`, `ZMod.unitsMap_def` and `hcast`, and none of these needs NeZero. If simp turns castHom into ZMod.cast, `ZMod.cast_intCast` (from memory: needs `m ∣ n` and CharP, not NeZero) handles it. The statement is also true when N = 0, where ZMod 0 = ℤ: reducing mod N preserves the lower-left entry being 0 and the image of the (1,1) entry. It can be dropped on its own or together with [NeZero (N * ℓ)].
- `[NeZero (N * ℓ)]` (theorem): The same reasoning applies: `GammaH (N*ℓ)`, `Gamma0 (N*ℓ)` and `ZMod.unitsMap (dvd_mul_right N ℓ)` all elaborate with no NeZero instance, and no step of the proof uses one. Only the next theorem in the file uses `ZMod.unitsMap_surjective`, which requires `[NeZero m]`; this proof does not. Mathematically the identity GammaH N H' ⊓ Gamma0 (N ℓ) = GammaH (N ℓ) (comap H') holds for all N and ℓ: an element of Gamma0 (Nℓ) is in Gamma0 N by reducing mod N, and its unit image in (ZMod N)ˣ is the reduction of its image in (ZMod (Nℓ))ˣ. This is also true when Nℓ = 0. It can be dropped on its own or together with [NeZero N].

### [`TateCurve.pointX_inv_mul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/Definitions/Def_TateCurve_XMultStructure.lean#L171-L175) — `flt-anthropic-0057`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0057.lean`

- `(hu0 : u ≠ 0)` (theorem): If u = 0, then u⁻¹ * v = 0⁻¹ * v = 0 (inv_zero, zero_mul) and u * v⁻¹ = 0 * v⁻¹ = 0. Both sides become `pointX q 0`, so the goal is closed by `subst; simp`. If u ≠ 0, the existing proof applies, and its only use of hu0 is to get `u * v⁻¹ ≠ 0` for `pointX_inv`. A proof is `by_cases hu : u = 0; · simp [hu]` followed by the current proof. This can be deleted together with hv0 and hq0.
- `(hv0 : v ≠ 0)` (theorem): If v = 0, then u⁻¹ * 0 = 0 and u * 0⁻¹ = u * 0 = 0 (inv_zero, mul_zero). Both sides are `pointX q 0`, so the case is closed by `simp`. Otherwise the existing proof applies, and its only use of hv0 is the non-zeroness argument passed to `pointX_inv`. This can be deleted independently of, or together with, hu0.
- `(hq0 : q ≠ 0)` (theorem): The only use of hq0 is to call `TateCurve.pointX_inv` (proved in P2M/Sol/S_TateCurve_pointX_inv.lean). That proof needs q ≠ 0 only to get `q ^ n * w ≠ 0` before applying `xfun_inv`. It can be redone without hq0. For each n, `xTerm q w⁻¹ (-n) = xfun ((q^n)⁻¹ * w⁻¹) = xfun ((q^n * w)⁻¹)`, using zpow_neg and mul_inv, which hold in any field even when q = 0. If `q^n * w = 0`, both sides are `xfun 0` by inv_zero. Otherwise `xfun_inv` (hypotheses w' ≠ 0 and w' ≠ 1, the second given by `hdiv n`) gives `xfun (q^n*w)`. The rest of that proof (`tsum_comp_neg`, `tsum_congr`, `congr 1` on `pointX`) does not use q ≠ 0. Concretely, when q = 0 only the n = 0 term is nonzero: `xfun w⁻¹ = xfun w` with w ≠ 1 from `hdiv 0`, or w = 0 trivially. So the statement still holds without hq0.

For ℓ = 0 we have `((0 : ℕ) : 𝓞 ℚ) = 0` and `Ideal.span {0} = ⊥`, so the `dif_pos` branch gives `placesOf 0 = ∅`. Then `hv : v ∈ placesOf 0` is false, and the conclusion follows from it. So for ℓ' = 0 the conclusion `v ∉ placesOf 0` holds trivially: after `subst`, use `simp [placesOf]`. If ℓ' ≠ 0, the existing proof through `disjoint_placesOf_of_coprime` applies unchanged. ### [`P2MW.S_IsDedekindDomain_HeightOneSpectrum_Extension_relIndex_adicCompletionIntegers_comap_sub_mulLeft_eq_absNorm_pow_min_of_ramificationIdx_eq_one.P2mLocalIndex.main`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_IsDedekindDomain_HeightOneSpectrum_Extension_relIndex_adicCompletionIntegers_comap_sub_mulLeft_eq_absNorm_pow_min_of_ramificationIdx_eq_one.lean#L843-L876) — `flt-anthropic-0059`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0059.lean`

- `(hres : ∃ y : w.1.adicCompletion L, ‖y‖ ≤ 1 ∧ ‖θ y - y‖ = 1)` (theorem): Implied by hw, hθ and hprime. `w : v.Extension (𝓞 L)` means `w.1.under (𝓞 K) = v`, so hw says w is unramified over v. The file proves `isField_V` from hw alone: V = k_v ⊗ O_w is a field of degree d = [L_w:K_v] over k_v, so the local extension is unramified with residue degree d. By hθ, θ has order d ≥ |Aut(L_w/K_v)|, so L_w/K_v is Galois (cyclic) with group generated by θ. In an unramified Galois extension the inertia group is trivial, so reduction is an isomorphism Gal(L_w/K_v) ≅ Gal(k_w/k_v), and θ maps to θ̄ of order d. Since d is prime (hprime), d ≥ 2 and θ̄ ≠ 1. Pick ȳ with θ̄ȳ ≠ ȳ and lift it to y ∈ O_w: then ‖y‖ ≤ 1, and θy − y is integral but not in the maximal ideal, hence a unit, so ‖θy − y‖ = 1. In the proof, hres is only used to get `sigmaV_ne_one`, which this argument supplies; the rest of the proof is unchanged.
- `(hprime : (Module.finrank (v.adicCompletion K) (w.1.adicCompletion L)).Prime)` (theorem): The proof uses hprime only for d > 0 (`hprime.pos`) and to get `orderOf_sigmaV` through `orderOf_eq_prime`. First, d > 0 holds anyway because L_w/K_v is finite-dimensional. Second, orderOf σ = d holds without primality. hθ makes L_w/K_v Galois with group ⟨θ⟩, and hw (via `isField_V`) makes it unramified. Then O_w = O_v[α] with α lifting a generator of the residue extension, and the minimal polynomial of α has separable reduction (finite fields). So θ^k(α) ≡ α mod ϖ forces θ^k(α) = α, i.e. θ^k = id. Hence σ^k ≠ 1 for 0 < k < d, and σ^d = 1 by `sigmaV_pow_finrank`. With orderOf σ = d, the rest of `finrank_ker_le_one` (fixed field of ⟨σ⟩ is k_v, Hilbert-90 style kernel bound) and the rest of the proof go through unchanged. Degenerate cases: hres forces θ ≠ id, so d ≥ 2; and in the d = 1 case the formula q^{min(s,m)} can be checked directly. Each of hres and hprime is claimed deletable on its own (the other kept); deleting both together was not checked.

So J₁ f ∈ F by `F.sub_mem`. The proof then goes through unchanged after `have hF₁ := ...`. Only one of hF₁, hF₂, hF₃ can be deleted at a time: each is derived from the other two.
- `(hF₂ : ∀ f ∈ F, J₂ f ∈ F)` (theorem): This follows from hF₁, hF₃ and h31. For f ∈ F, h31 gives J₂ f = J₃ (J₁ f) - J₁ (J₃ f). Both terms lie in F by hF₁ and hF₃, so J₂ f ∈ F by `F.sub_mem`. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hF₁ or hF₃.
- `(hF₃ : ∀ f ∈ F, J₃ f ∈ F)` (theorem): This follows from hF₁, hF₂ and h12. For f ∈ F, h12 gives J₃ f = J₁ (J₂ f) - J₂ (J₁ f). Both terms lie in F by hF₁ and hF₂, so J₃ f ∈ F by `F.sub_mem`. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hF₁ or hF₂.
- `(hskew₃ : ∀ x ∈ F, ∀ y ∈ F, B (J₃ x) y = -B x (J₃ y))` (theorem): This follows from hskew₁, hskew₂, h12, hlin, hsymm and the invariance hypotheses. The proof already derives hBsub (subtraction in the first argument, from hlin) and hBsub' (subtraction in the second argument, from hlin and hsymm). For x, y ∈ F, all of J₁J₂x, J₂J₁x, J₁J₂y and J₂J₁y lie in F. Then B (J₃ x) y = B (J₁J₂x - J₂J₁x) y = B (J₁J₂x) y - B (J₂J₁x) y = -B (J₂x) (J₁y) + B (J₁x) (J₂y) = B x (J₂J₁y) - B x (J₁J₂y) = -B x (J₁J₂y - J₂J₁y) = -B x (J₃ y). The steps use h12, hBsub, hskew₁ and hskew₂ twice each, hBsub', and h12 again. Proving this right after hBsub' leaves the rest of the proof unchanged. Only one of hskew₁, hskew₂, hskew₃ can be deleted at a time.
- `(hskew₁ : ∀ x ∈ F, ∀ y ∈ F, B (J₁ x) y = -B x (J₁ y))` (theorem): This is the same argument with h23 (J₁ = J₂J₃ - J₃J₂ on F): B (J₁ x) y = B (J₂J₃x) y - B (J₃J₂x) y = B x (J₃J₂y) - B x (J₂J₃y) = -B x (J₁ y). It uses hskew₂, hskew₃, hBsub and hBsub', which come from hlin and hsymm. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hskew₂ or hskew₃.
- `(hskew₂ : ∀ x ∈ F, ∀ y ∈ F, B (J₂ x) y = -B x (J₂ y))` (theorem): This is the same argument with h31 (J₂ = J₃J₁ - J₁J₃ on F): B (J₂ x) y = B (J₃J₁x) y - B (J₁J₃x) y = B x (J₁J₃y) - B x (J₃J₁y) = -B x (J₂ y). It uses hskew₁, hskew₃, hBsub and hBsub'. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hskew₁ or hskew₃.

Each of those lemmas gets its arguments explicitly (Mdl₁, c₁, e₁, he₁, morphisms, sets), and a Prop hypothesis like this one cannot be found by instance search. So the same proof works after deleting it.
- `(hpM : ¬ p ∣ M)` (theorem): A Prop that nothing else in the statement mentions. The proof never refers to it and no lemma it calls picks it up, so the proof is unchanged without it.
- `(hζ : IsPrimitiveRoot ζ p)` (theorem): A Prop that nothing else in the statement mentions (ζ itself stays, since hζA uses it). The proof never uses hζ.
- `(hK : K = ModularCurve.laurentBaseChange L (ModularCurve.x1FunctionField (M * p)))` (theorem): An equation that nothing else in the statement mentions. K is a variable that elaborates without it, and the proof never rewrites with or refers to hK. The argument is entirely about the curve model Mdl₁, the closed immersion i₁ and finite sets of points, so it holds for any K.
- `(hj : ((j : LaurentSeries L)) = ModularCurve.coeffEmb L ModularCurve.jq)` (theorem): A Prop that nothing else in the statement mentions and that the proof never uses. Only j itself, and possibly the Fact (j ≠ 0) instance, are needed to form modelTo and ιFin.
- `(hAp : (p : A) ∈ IsLocalRing.maximalIdeal A)` (theorem): A Prop that nothing else in the statement mentions and that the proof never uses.
- `(hζA : ∃ z : A, algebraMap A L z = ζ)` (theorem): A Prop that nothing else in the statement mentions and that the proof never uses.
- `(hcover : ∀ z : ↥(pullback (ModularCurve.TwoChart.modelTo A (↥K) j) (specMap A k)), z ∈ Set.range i₁.1.base ∨ z ∈ Set.range i₂.1.base)` (theorem): A Prop that nothing else in the statement mentions and that the proof never uses. The argument only needs the set of crossing points R to be finite, which comes from hn and hn0, and U to be nonempty, which comes from hne₁. It never needs i₁ and i₂ to cover the special fibre.
- `(hred : IsReduced (pullback i₁.1 i₂.1))` (theorem): A Prop that nothing else in the statement mentions and that the proof never uses. Finiteness of the pullback comes from Nat.finite_of_card_ne_zero using hn and hn0 alone. IsReduced is a class, but nothing in the proof searches for an IsReduced instance on that pullback.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_exists_variableChange_weightOne_tateBase_mem_laurentBaseChange_and_cuspData_mem.R4GC.qC_eq_zero_iff`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_exists_variableChange_weightOne_tateBase_mem_laurentBaseChange_and_cuspData_mem.lean#L1440-L1442) — `flt-anthropic-0104`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0104.lean`

- `[NeZero m]` (variable): The theorem takes `[NeZero m]` only because it is an instance-implicit section variable and `m` appears in the statement. No part of the statement needs it: `qC` (line 1424), `Γ m M'` (line 1187) and `GH m M'` (line 1185, `CohCarrier.GammaH (m^2*M') (levelH m M')`) are definitions whose bodies use no NeZero instance. The proof uses Mathlib's `ModularForm.qExpansion_eq_zero_iff (hh : 0 < h) (hΓ : h ∈ Γ.strictPeriods) f`, which needs only that 1 is a strict period, plus `HahnSeries.ofPowerSeries_injective`. The local `one_mem_strictPeriods` (line 1213) also takes `[NeZero m]` as a section variable, but its proof does not use it. It rewrites with Mathlib's `Subgroup.strictPeriods_eq_zmultiples_one_of_T_mem`, which needs only `T ∈ Γ`, and that comes from `ModularCurve.translation_mem_GammaH`, proved for arbitrary `M : ℕ` and `H : Subgroup (ZMod M)ˣ` with no NeZero (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_XH.lean, line 20). So for m = 0 the theorem still holds: T lies in GH 0 M', 1 is a strict period, and the q-expansion is injective on modular forms. Fix: add `omit [NeZero m] in` before the theorem, and either add the same `omit` before `one_mem_strictPeriods` or inline its two-line proof.

### [`P2MW.S_CuspForm_heckeLocal_apply_eq_pi_U_of_forall_point_apply_eq_unitRoot_of_isAbsolutelyIrreducible.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_CuspForm_heckeLocal_apply_eq_pi_U_of_forall_point_apply_eq_unitRoot_of_isAbsolutelyIrreducible.lean#L32-L223) — `flt-anthropic-0121`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0121.lean`

- `(hθ : ∀ t : CuspForm.heckeAlgebra N 2 (↑S : Set ℕ),
      θ t = θ' (Subalgebra.inclusion (CuspForm.heckeAlgebra_mono (Finset.coe_subset.mpr hS₀)) t))` (theorem): The proof never refers to `hθ`. The only name in the proof that starts with `hθ` is `hθ'U`, which is a different hypothesis. No other binder or the conclusion mentions `hθ`, so deleting it does not affect elaboration: `θ` is still bound and still determines the types of `Ψ`, `hΨ`, `u` and `hu`.

The proof does not need it. It gets the points `χ i` from `CuspForm.heckeLocal.exists_points_jointly_injective_of_charpoly_frobenius_eq_of_isAbsolutelyIrreducible`. I read that lemma's signature: it takes hp2, hp𝒪, ρbar, habs, S, S₀, hS, hpS, hS₀, N, hNS, hNS₀, hNp, hunr, hst, hsq, hord, θ', hT, hU0, hU1 and hTp, and does not involve θ or hθ. The proof then builds `χS` and `χSp` by composing with `χ i`, `Ψ` and inclusions, and calls `CuspForm.exists_isNewform_of_point_of_up_dvd` with p, N, S, hNS, χS, hpN, up and data built from `χSp`. The compatibility of `Ψ` with the Hecke operators comes from `hΨ` (it is used when proving `hiota`), not from `hθ`. The rest is `hu`, `hθ'U` and ring arithmetic (`linear_combination`, facts about the maximal ideal). None of these tactics look at `hθ`: `simp` and `linear_combination` are not given it, and the `congr 1` goal only compares Hecke operators that differ in proof arguments. So the existing proof compiles unchanged without `hθ`, and the theorem stays true.

### [`P2MW.S_NumberField_TateGlobal_zetaIntegral_meromorphic_continuation_fe.M4aD2Skeleton.zetaIntegral_eq_setIntegral_theta'`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_NumberField_TateGlobal_zetaIntegral_meromorphic_continuation_fe.lean#L6363-L6392) — `flt-anthropic-0122`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0122.lean`

- `(hD'm : MeasurableSet D')` (theorem): The proof uses hD'm only in its call to `MeasureTheory.setIntegral_congr_fun hD'm`, and that step does not need it. The pointwise identity there holds for every x, with no condition on x ∈ D'. `hterm` is proved for an arbitrary x using only `chi_mul_principal`, `ideleNorm_mul_principal`, `mul_comm` and `mul_assoc`. After `tsum_congr hterm, tsum_mul_right`, the left side is `(∑' a, f ↑(x * ↑a)) * (χ x * ‖x‖^s)`. This is definitionally `ThetaSum F f x * (...)`, since `ThetaSum` is defined as exactly that tsum (line 5808), which is why the final `rfl` closes the goal. So the two integrands are equal as functions. The step can therefore be replaced by `congr 1; funext x` (or `integral_congr_ae (Filter.Eventually.of_forall ...)` for the restricted measure), which needs no measurability of D'. The other step, `M4aD2Glue.integral_eq_setIntegral_tsum_smul` (line 5498), takes only `IsFundamentalDomain G D μ` and `Integrable f μ`, not `MeasurableSet D`. D' appears elsewhere in the statement only through hD' and the set integral, neither of which needs hD'm to elaborate.
- `[BorelSpace (AdeleRing (𝓞 F) F)]` (theorem): No other part of the statement needs this instance. `ideleNorm` (Def_NumberField_TateGlobalZeta.lean:19) is `distribHaarChar (AdeleRing ..) x` with no measurable-space argument. `zetaIntegral` takes only a measurable space on the units. `schwartzBruhat` and `ThetaSum` take no measure-theoretic instances. `NumberField.TateGlobal.integrable_zetaIntegrand` (Theorems/Thm_NumberField_TateGlobal_integrable_zetaIntegrand.lean) takes only `MeasurableSpace`/`BorelSpace` on the units. The only place the proof uses the instance is `ideleNorm_mul_principal F a x`, whose statement `ideleNorm F (↑a * x) = ideleNorm F x` does not involve the measurable structure. It can be called with a locally chosen Borel structure, `@ideleNorm_mul_principal F _ _ (borel _) ⟨rfl⟩ a x`, which proves the same equality. So the theorem stays provable without this instance. (`[MeasurableSpace (AdeleRing (𝓞 F) F)]` is then also unused, but it cannot be deleted while the BorelSpace binder, which mentions it, is still present, so only the BorelSpace binder is reported as deletable on its own.)

### [`P2MW.S_NumberField_AdelicFourier_exists_forall_setIntegral_norm_le_one_tsum_norm_apply_smul_vecMul_mul_rpow_le_mul_archHeight_pow_of_mem_integralWindowedSiegelSet_rat.NumberField.AdelicFourier.GodementSiegelEstimate.real_bound`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_NumberField_AdelicFourier_exists_forall_setIntegral_norm_le_one_tsum_norm_apply_smul_vecMul_mul_rpow_le_mul_archHeight_pow_of_mem_integralWindowedSiegelSet_rat.lean#L337-L423) — `flt-anthropic-0123`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0123.lean`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): The statement stays true without the hypothesis. Under the remaining hypotheses (1 ≤ r, 0 < α, 0 < δ), the left side is C times three factors: (1 + (r*α)⁻¹^2) > 0, (1 + (r*δ)⁻¹^2) > 0, and min 1 ((r*α)⁻¹^N) + min 1 ((r*δ)⁻¹^N) ≥ 0. Call their product X ≥ 0. If C ≤ 0, the left side is ≤ 0. Taking B = 0 then works: 0 ≤ B holds and the right side is 0. Proof without hC: case-split on 0 ≤ C, using the existing proof in the positive case and B = 0 otherwise (the left side is nonpositive by mul_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg-style reasoning). Alternatively, use the witness max C 0 * (1 + e^2) * (1 + D⁻¹) * (e^N + d^N), which is nonnegative, and first bound C * X ≤ max C 0 * X since X ≥ 0; the existing calc then runs with max C 0 in place of C. The other hypotheses are genuinely needed when C > 0. Without hc, H can be arbitrarily small, so α → 0 makes the left side unbounded while the right side stays near B. Without hD, the constraint D ≤ αδ is vacuous: fix H = c, take α = cδ and let δ → 0.

### [`P2MW.S_TateCurve_ks17_D3_exports.TateCurve.sum_mul_Fz_Fz_eq_sum_lines_nat`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_TateCurve_ks17_D3_exports.lean#L108-L111) — `flt-anthropic-0124`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0124.lean`

- `[IsUltrametricDist K]` (theorem): Only a field is needed to state this. `TateCurve.Fz` is defined in Definitions/Def_TateCurve_DefectLines.lean (line 420), in a section whose only assumptions are `[Field K] (u : K)`: `Fz u a = u ^ a + u ^ (-a) - 2`. So nothing in the statement needs the ultrametric instance to elaborate. The original proof (P2M/Sol/S_TateCurve_ks17_C3_exports.lean:224) uses only `ring`, `Finset.sum_congr`, `Finset.sum_mul` and the helper `sum_mul_Fz_eq_sum_lines_nat`. That helper (Def_TateCurve_DefectLines.lean:1349) is stated with only `[NontriviallyNormedField K]`. It groups the sum by fibers with `Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to` over `range (N+1)` and drops the 0 fiber using `Fz_zero` (`u^0+u^0-2=0`, true in any field). So the same proof goes through without this instance. Degenerate cases: if S is empty, both sides are 0. Terms with g x = 0 or h x = 0 vanish because Fz _ 0 = 0. If N = 0, the bounds force every g x = 0, so both sides are 0. This instance and [CompleteSpace K] can each be deleted, and so can both together.
- `[CompleteSpace K]` (theorem): Same reasoning as for [IsUltrametricDist K]. `Fz` needs only `[Field K]`, which `NontriviallyNormedField K` provides. The proof is a finite rearrangement of sums: `Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to`, `Finset.sum_mul`, `ring`, plus `Fz_zero`, which is the identity u^0 + u^0 - 2 = 0. It never uses completeness. The helper `sum_mul_Fz_eq_sum_lines_nat` (Def_TateCurve_DefectLines.lean:1349) is already stated with only `[NontriviallyNormedField K]`. So the theorem stays true and the existing proof works with this instance removed, whether or not [IsUltrametricDist K] is also removed.

### [`P2MW.S_WeierstrassCurve_exists_prePsi_eq_expand.WeierstrassCurve.Universal.Ψ₂Sq_𝓦p_ne_zero`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_WeierstrassCurve_exists_prePsi_eq_expand.lean#L273-L277) — `flt-anthropic-0125`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0125.lean`

- `(hp2 : p ≠ 2)` (theorem): Mathlib defines Ψ₂Sq = C 4 * X^3 + C b₂ * X^2 + C (2*b₄) * X + C b₆, with b₂ = a₁^2 + 4*a₂. For 𝓦p p = ⟨X 0, X 1, X 2, X 3, X 4⟩ over Rp p = MvPolynomial (Fin 5) (ZMod p), the degree-2 coefficient of Ψ₂Sq is X 0 ^ 2 + 4 * X 1. Its MvPolynomial coefficient at Finsupp.single 0 2 is 1 + 4*0 = 1, which is nonzero because ZMod p is nontrivial for any prime p, including p = 2. So Ψ₂Sq ≠ 0 for every prime p. Only the current proof needs hp2, because it uses the degree-3 coefficient 4, which vanishes when p = 2. Replacement proof: take Polynomial.coeff _ 2 of h and simp to X 0 ^ 2 + 4 * X 1 = 0, then take MvPolynomial.coeff (Finsupp.single 0 2) of both sides to get 1 = 0 in ZMod p, contradicting nontriviality (this uses Fact p.Prime). Fact p.Prime itself cannot be dropped: for p = 1, ZMod 1 is the zero ring and Ψ₂Sq = 0.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_supersingularProlongation_exists_finite_generators_regular_off_ends_residueField_of_eq_three_of_dvd.AlgebraicCurve.HolRingFGAux.exists_fin_generators`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_supersingularProlongation_exists_finite_generators_regular_off_ends_residueField_of_eq_three_of_dvd.lean#L590-L610) — `flt-anthropic-0132`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0132.lean`

- `(hN : N.Nonempty)` (theorem): hN is only used to call holRing_fg (via exists_fn, which needs a point of N). For N = ∅ the statement still holds with n = 0: the first conjunct is vacuous (Fin 0 is empty), and for the second, if f = 0 it lies in any subalgebra. If f ≠ 0 and f is in every place's valuation ring, then 0 ≤ v.ord f for all v (mem_toValuationSubring_iff_ord / ord_nonneg_iff in the library). HasPrincipalDivisors (a parent of IsCurveOver) gives a divisor D with D v = v.ord f and degree D = 0. Since k is algebraically closed, every place has degree 1 (IsCurveOver.deg_eq_one_of_isAlgClosed), so degree D = Σ_{v ∈ support} D v (degree_eq_sum in the same file). A zero sum of nonnegative terms forces every v.ord f = 0. Then AlgebraicCurve.Place.mem_range_algebraMap_of_forall_ord_eq_zero_of_isAlgClosed, applied to the separating transcendental j from IsCurveOver.exists_separating_transcendental (which needs PerfectField k, from IsAlgClosed, and EssFiniteType), gives f ∈ range (algebraMap k K) ⊆ Algebra.adjoin k ∅. So: split on N.eq_empty_or_nonempty, use this argument with n = 0 in the empty case, and keep the existing proof otherwise.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_exists_monic_natDegree_le_aeval_jqModC_eq_zero.ModularCurve.map_eval₂_aeval_toRingHom`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_exists_monic_natDegree_le_aeval_jqModC_eq_zero.lean#L18-L24) — `flt-anthropic-0137`

- `[Algebra ℤ A]` (theorem): `[CommRing A]` already gives an `Algebra ℤ A` instance through Mathlib's global instance `algebraInt`. Mathlib also proves `int_algebra_subsingleton : Subsingleton (Algebra ℤ R)` (Mathlib/Algebra/Algebra/Basic.lean), so every `Algebra ℤ A` structure is the canonical one. ### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_JHNeronObjectAtP_toricPts_le_and_finPts_le_and_natCard_toricPts_mul_natCard_finPts_eq_of_coprime.C2Count.natCard_finPts`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_JHNeronObjectAtP_toricPts_le_and_finPts_le_and_natCard_toricPts_mul_natCard_finPts_eq_of_coprime.lean#L214-L257) — `flt-anthropic-0143`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0143.lean`

- `(hm : 0 < m)` (theorem): `hm` follows from `hmp : m.Coprime p` together with the variable `[Fact p.Prime]`. That variable is an argument of the theorem because it is instance-implicit and `p` appears in the statement. If `m = 0`, then `Nat.coprime_zero_left : Coprime 0 n ↔ n = 1` (Lean core, Init/Data/Nat/Coprime.lean, a @[simp] lemma) turns `hmp` into `p = 1`, which contradicts `(Fact.out : p.Prime).ne_one`. So `m ≠ 0`, hence `0 < m`. The proof uses `hm` only to build the witnesses `⟨m, hm, hpm, _⟩` passed to `hinj` and `hsurj`. Adding `have hm : 0 < m := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero (by rintro rfl; exact (Fact.out : p.Prime).ne_one ((Nat.coprime_zero_left p).mp hmp))` at the start lets the rest of the proof go through unchanged. `hm` is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_contDiff_and_finsum_smoothedFundamental_eq_one.ModularCurve.SmoothedFundamentalProof.finite_support_smul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_contDiff_and_finsum_smoothedFundamental_eq_one.lean#L435-L442) — `flt-anthropic-0144`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0144.lean`

- `[Γ.FiniteIndex]` (variable): `ModularCurve.smoothedFundamental Γ T z` is a `finsum` over `SL(2,ℤ) ⧸ Γ` (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_SmoothedFundamental.lean, line 170). That definition needs no finiteness; with infinite index the finsum may take the junk value 0, which only shrinks the support. The current proof uses FiniteIndex only through `carrier`/`isCompact_carrierH`/`mem_carrier_of_ne_zero`, i.e. through the Fintype instance on the quotient. Alternative proof without it: let S = {δ : SL(2,ℤ) | puCut T (mob δ τ) ≠ 0}, which is finite by `hasFiniteSupport_puCut_mob T τ` (no Γ, no FiniteIndex). If smoothedFundamental Γ T (γ•τ) ≠ 0, then by the contrapositive of `finsum_eq_zero_of_forall_eq_zero` (additive form of `finprod_eq_one_of_forall_eq_one`) some coset q has puCut T (mob q.out (γ•τ)) ≠ 0. By `coe_smul` and `mob_mob` this equals puCut T (mob (q.out * γ) τ), so δ := q.out*γ ∈ S. Since γ ∈ Γ, (q.out)⁻¹δ = γ ∈ Γ, so ⟦δ⟧ = q (left cosets) and γ = (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹ * δ. Hence the support lies in the image of the finite set S under δ ↦ (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹δ and is finite. Degenerate cases: infinite index only shrinks the support; when Γ is trivial the support still injects into S.

### [`P2MW.S_GoodReductionJacobian_RelativeGroupLaw_isFinite_and_flat_of_surjective_of_isFinite_pullback_snd.P2mFlatIsog.ringKrullDim_stalk_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_GoodReductionJacobian_RelativeGroupLaw_isFinite_and_flat_of_surjective_of_isFinite_pullback_snd.lean#L246-L300) — `flt-anthropic-0150`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0150.lean`

- `[IsIntegral Y]` (theorem): The statement stays true without `[IsIntegral Y]`. The only place the proof uses it is to show that φ = h.app V : A := Γ(Y,V) → B := Γ(X,h⁻¹V) is injective (through `basicOpen_eq_bot_iff`, which needs Y reduced), and to treat A as a domain in `height_comap_eq`.

Without the hypothesis, an affine-local argument works. Let I = ker φ. Since h is surjective, the induced map Spec B → Spec A is surjective, so every prime of A contains I. Hence I ⊆ nilradical(A); in fact the earlier basic-open argument shows directly that ker φ consists of nilpotents. Because B is a domain (X is integral and h⁻¹V is a nonempty affine open), A/I embeds into B, so A/I is a domain. Also:
- A/I is finitely generated over K, as a quotient of A, which is finite type through fY (`exists_algebra_finiteType`, which does not need integrality).
- B is integral over A/I.

So `height_comap_eq K` applies to the injective integral map A/I → B. It gives ht(p/I) = ht(q), where q = px and p = py = comap q. Since I lies in every prime of A, the primes of A below p correspond to the primes of A/I below p/I, so ht p = ht(p/I). The two `IsLocalization.AtPrime ... ringKrullDim_eq_height` rewrites need neither integrality nor reducedness of Y, so they still finish the proof.

Degenerate cases: Y is nonempty because it contains h.base x. Y is irreducible as the image of the irreducible X under the surjection h, so nothing else is lost.

Geometrically, h factors through Y_red, and the local rings of Y and Y_red differ only by nilpotents, which leaves the Krull dimension unchanged.

### [`P2MW.S_CuspidalType_sum_character_mul_character_inv.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_CuspidalType_sum_character_mul_character_inv.lean#L10-L30) — `flt-anthropic-0154`

The proof never uses it either. It builds `IsIrreducible ρ` from `hirr` and `Nontrivial V`, gets `Invertible (Nat.card (GL2 q) : K)` from `CharZero`, then applies `Representation.char_orthonormal ρ ρ`. That lemma only assumes `[Fintype G] [Invertible (Nat.card G : k)] [IsAlgClosed k] [IsIrreducible ρ] [IsIrreducible σ]`; it derives finite-dimensionality internally via `ρ.asModuleEquiv.finiteDimensional`. On paper it also follows from the other hypotheses: for any nonzero v (V is nontrivial), the span of the finite orbit G·v is a nonzero subrepresentation, so by `hirr` it is all of V, hence V is finite-dimensional. So the theorem holds and the same proof goes through without this binder.

The statement is a purely algebraic identity about tensors, so it stays true and provable with the same proof once this instance is omitted. The pair (0, 0) works without it: q' 0 = 0 = q'' 0 (map_zero). Then `obtain ⟨b, -, huniq⟩ := hpb 0 0 (by simp)`. Both 0 (map_zero) and b₁ - b₂ (map_sub with h', h'') have images 0 under p' and p'', so uniqueness gives b₁ - b₂ = b = 0, and sub_eq_zero gives b₁ = b₂. So the statement holds for any ring homs satisfying hpb, whether or not the square commutes; no degenerate cases arise because 0 always exists. ### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicGeometry_exists_isProper_surjective_isOpenImmersion_isIntegral_isIso_morphismRestrict_of_isIntegral.SolCHint.ChartData.V_eq_O`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicGeometry_exists_isProper_surjective_isOpenImmersion_isIntegral_isIso_morphismRestrict_of_isIntegral.lean#L489-L504) — `flt-anthropic-0182`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0182.lean`

- `[IsNoetherianRing A]` (variable): ChartData f only needs [CommRing A]. In V_eq_O the Noetherian hypotheses are used only through isNoetherian_X, which makes Uo a Noetherian space and so gives QuasiCompact D.ψ. That is what makes X' = ψ.image integral (Mathlib's `IsDominant f.toImage` instance needs `[QuasiCompact f]`), and that feeds irreducibleSpace_X'/isIntegral_X', IsDominant ψk (used by ak_eq_bk) and the preconnectedness of O k. QuasiCompact D.ψ also follows from IsSeparated f alone. Each U k is affine: c k is a closed immersion into an affine Spec, closed immersions are IsAffineHom (ClosedImmersion.lean:147), and `isAffine_of_isAffineHom` applies; so U k is compact. X is quasi-separated (f separated, target affine). So Uo, a finite intersection of compact opens, is compact; X itself is compact as the finite union of the U k, which covers m = 0. Then Uo.ι is quasi-compact by `quasiCompact_of_compactSpace`. pullback.fst f D.prπ is proper (as in the `IsProper D.π` instance), hence quasi-separated, so `QuasiCompact.of_comp` with ψ ≫ pullback.fst = Uo.ι gives QuasiCompact ψ. With that, the rest of the proof goes through unchanged; it uses IsSeparated f and IsIntegral X but not this hypothesis. Removable on its own, and together with [LocallyOfFiniteType f] and [QuasiCompact f].
- `[LocallyOfFiniteType f]` (variable): Same argument. Its only role is in isNoetherian_X (via LocallyOfFiniteType.isLocallyNoetherian), which gives QuasiCompact D.ψ. That can be derived instead from IsSeparated f: the U k are affine because c k is a closed immersion into an affine scheme (`isAffine_of_isAffineHom`); X is quasi-separated, so Uo is compact; Uo.ι is quasi-compact (`quasiCompact_of_compactSpace`); pullback.fst f prπ is proper, hence quasi-separated; and `QuasiCompact.of_comp` finishes. The rest of the proof (integrality of X', ak_eq_bk, isClosedImmersion_ιΩ, properness of ek, the clopen argument) does not otherwise use this hypothesis.
- `[QuasiCompact f]` (variable): Same argument. It is used only in isNoetherian_X (to get CompactSpace X), which in turn gives QuasiCompact D.ψ. Here X is already compact, being covered by the finitely many U k, each affine because c k is a closed immersion into an affine Spec. QuasiCompact ψ follows from IsSeparated f as follows: Uo is compact in the quasi-separated X, so Uo.ι is quasi-compact (`quasiCompact_of_compactSpace`); pullback.fst f prπ is proper, hence quasi-separated; and `QuasiCompact.of_comp` applies to ψ ≫ pullback.fst = Uo.ι. No other step of V_eq_O needs this hypothesis.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_RationalFunctionField_stichtenothGenusExists_of_ratFunc_tower.AlgebraicCurve.AlgebraicCurve.adeleBddPrincipal_eq_comap`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_RationalFunctionField_stichtenothGenusExists_of_ratFunc_tower.lean#L791-L793) — `flt-anthropic-0183`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0183.lean`

- `[HasPrincipalDivisors K F]` (theorem): The statement elaborates without the instance. `adeleBddPrincipal`, `adeleBdd`, `globalSub` and `adeleSpace` (Definitions/Def_AlgebraicCurve_AdelicIndex.lean) are all defined without it. Write S = adeleSpace K F, A = adeleBdd D and B = globalSub K F. The claim is comap S.subtype A ⊔ comap S.subtype B = comap S.subtype (A ⊔ B). This only needs A ≤ S, which is `adeleBdd_le_adeleSpace` and is proved with no instance, since S is defined as the supremum of the adeleBdd submodules. The instance is used only through `globalSub_le_adeleSpace` (B ≤ S, via `diagonal_mem_adeleSpace`), and that inclusion is not needed. Proof without it: (≤) is `sup_le (Submodule.comap_mono le_sup_left) (Submodule.comap_mono le_sup_right)`. (≥) take x ∈ S with (x : Place K F → F) ∈ A ⊔ B. By `Submodule.mem_sup` we get x = a + b with a ∈ A and b ∈ B. Then a ∈ S by `adeleBdd_le_adeleSpace`, so b = x - a ∈ S. Hence ⟨a, _⟩ lies in comap A, ⟨b, _⟩ lies in comap B, and their sum is x, so x is in the left-hand side. Equivalently, this is the modular law (`sup_inf_assoc_of_le` in Mathlib/Order/ModularLattice.lean, for the modular lattice of submodules) transported along the subtype. No degenerate cases arise: the argument is pure linear algebra and works for any D, K and F.

The proof does a case split on whether f = 0. If f ≠ 0, the existing proof applies unchanged. If f = 0, then hP gives `P v = v.ord 0 = 0` for every place v, so `P = 0` by `Finsupp.ext`. Then `E - P = E - 0 = E` by `sub_zero`, and `ell (E - P) = ell E` holds by rfl. So the statement stays true and provable without hf0. The case f = 0 is the degenerate one, and the junk value ord 0 = 0 makes it hold.

The proof uses it only to supply `NeZero (N * q)` to `deg_eq_one_modularFunctionFieldBar`. Without it, `N = 0` is allowed and the level `N * q` is 0. The statement still holds because every place of `modularFunctionFieldBar 0` has degree 1. `B2Deg.deg_eq_one_of_isAlgebraic_adjoin` (in P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_deg_eq_one_modularFunctionFieldBar.lean) gives `w.deg = 1` for any field that is algebraic over ℚ̄(t), using only that ℚ̄ is algebraically closed. It contains j, by the `jq_mem_full` variant proved via `jqd_mem_full N (one_dvd N)` with `NeZero N` omitted. Then `B2Deg.deg_eq_one_of_isAlgebraic_adjoin` gives degree 1 for every place. For q ≠ 0 the existing lemma applies directly once `NeZero (N * q)` is available. ### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_DRModel_exists_curveModel_closedImmersion_pair_pFibre_cover_levelSet_singleton.GLUECI.eq_span_tInvChart_of_mem`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_DRModel_exists_curveModel_closedImmersion_pair_pFibre_cover_levelSet_singleton.lean#L2727-L2734) — `flt-anthropic-0199`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0199.lean`

- `(ht : Transcendental κ t)` (variable): chartRing κ S is the integral closure of `Algebra.adjoin κ S` in the field L, so it is a domain, and tInvChart κ t = ⟨t⁻¹, _⟩. Suppose t is not transcendental, so it is algebraic over κ (the Fact t ≠ 0 is omitted, so t = 0 is allowed). (1) If t ≠ 0: t is algebraic, hence integral, over the field κ. By `IsIntegral.tower_top` it is integral over adjoin κ {t⁻¹}, so t ∈ chartRing κ {t⁻¹}. Then tInvChart κ t * ⟨t,_⟩ = 1, so tInvChart is a unit. Having it in the prime ideal 𝔮 contradicts IsPrime.ne_top, so `h` is false and the statement is vacuous. (2) If t = 0: t⁻¹ = 0 and tInvChart = 0. By hchartInv every element of the ring is aeval 0 f = algebraMap κ L (f.coeff 0), so every nonzero element is a unit and the prime 𝔮 must be ⊥ = Ideal.span {0}. The conclusion holds. So the new proof is: if t is transcendental, use the existing argument (eq_span_of_pow_mem with transcendental_inv); otherwise use the argument above. hchartInv cannot be dropped: with L = κ(u) and t = u⁻², chartRing κ {u²} = κ[u] and the prime (u) ∋ u² is not span{u²}.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_exists_mul_eq_of_height_one_of_natCast_mem_level.RoadA.coe_τ`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_exists_mul_eq_of_height_one_of_natCast_mem_level.lean#L325-L326) — `flt-anthropic-0219`

### [`P2MW.S_PowerSeries_dvd_of_forall_taylorShift_order_le.CuspDisc.Bdd.mul_small`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_PowerSeries_dvd_of_forall_taylorShift_order_le.lean#L132-L135) — `flt-anthropic-0231`
