- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): Implied by hP. `Bdd C P` unfolds to `∀ k, ‖coeff k P‖ ≤ C` (line 20), so `hP 0` with `norm_nonneg` gives `0 ≤ C`. The proof becomes: `have hC : 0 ≤ C := (norm_nonneg _).trans (hP 0)`, then the same `rw [mul_comm ...]; exact hQ.mul hρ0 hρ1 hC' hC hP`. This holds whichever of the other removable hypotheses are also deleted.
- `(hC' : 0 ≤ C')` (theorem): Implied by hQ. `Small ρ C' m Q` unfolds to `∀ k, ‖coeff k Q‖ ≤ C' * ρ ^ (m - k)` (line 18). At `k = m` the exponent is `m - m = 0`, so `hQ m` reads `‖coeff m Q‖ ≤ C' * 1`, and `norm_nonneg` gives `0 ≤ C'`. Rebuild it with `have hC' : 0 ≤ C' := by have := hQ m; simp at this; exact (norm_nonneg _).trans this`, then call `Small.mul` as before.
- `(hρ0 : 0 ≤ ρ)` (theorem): The statement still holds for ρ < 0; the proof splits on the sign of ρ. If 0 ≤ ρ, the original proof works. If ρ < 0 and m = 0, every exponent `0 - k` is 0, so `small_zero_iff` turns hQ into `Bdd C' Q` and the goal into `Bdd (C*C') (P*Q)`. `Bdd.mul hC hC' hP hQ` (line 137, no ρ involved) closes it. If ρ < 0 and m ≥ 1, `hQ (m-1)` gives `0 ≤ ‖coeff (m-1) Q‖ ≤ C' * ρ ^ 1 = C' * ρ`, so `C' ≤ 0`. With `0 ≤ C'` (hC', or derived from `hQ m`) this gives `C' = 0`. Then every bound in hQ is `0 * ... = 0`, so all coefficients of Q vanish, Q = 0 and P * Q = 0. The goal `‖coeff n 0‖ ≤ C * 0 * ρ ^ (m - n)` becomes `0 ≤ 0`. By contrast, hρ1 is needed: ρ = 2, m = 1, C = C' = 1, P = X, Q = 2 satisfy hP and hQ, but coeff 1 (P*Q) = 2 > 1 = 1·1·2⁰.

### [`P2MW.S_Algebra_exists_isStandardEtale_mvPolynomial_localizationAway_of_smooth_of_kaehlerDifferential.EtaleCoordK1a.rankAtStalk_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Algebra_exists_isStandardEtale_mvPolynomial_localizationAway_of_smooth_of_kaehlerDifferential.lean#L13-L24) — `flt-anthropic-0236`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0236.lean`

- `[Module.Flat S M]` (theorem): rankAtStalk M p is defined as finrank over Localization.AtPrime p of LocalizedModule p.primeCompl M. S is a domain, so 0 ∈ p and every element of p.primeCompl is nonzero, hence a non-zero-divisor: p.primeCompl ≤ nonZeroDivisors S. Two Mathlib lemmas in LinearAlgebra/Dimension/Localization.lean need only this inclusion, with no flatness or finiteness assumption. IsLocalization.rank_eq gives that the rank over the localized ring equals the rank over S. IsLocalizedModule.rank_eq (with f := LocalizedModule.mkLinearMap) gives that the localized module has the same rank as M, which is n by hrank. Module.finrank_eq_of_rank_eq then yields finrank = n. The existing proof only uses Flat and FinitePresentation in its first step, Module.isLocallyConstant_rankAtStalk plus connectedness, which moves from p to the generic point ⊥. Dropping that step and running the remaining argument directly at p (replace ⊥ by p.asIdeal; prove hle with mem_nonZeroDivisors_of_ne_zero and p.asIdeal.zero_mem) proves the statement without this hypothesis. Flat and FinitePresentation can even be deleted together.
- `[Module.FinitePresentation S M]` (theorem): Same argument as for Module.Flat. In a domain, p.primeCompl ≤ nonZeroDivisors S for every prime p, because 0 ∈ p. Hence IsLocalization.rank_eq and IsLocalizedModule.rank_eq, which assume only this inclusion, give that the rank of LocalizedModule p.primeCompl M over Localization.AtPrime p equals Module.rank S M = n. Module.finrank_eq_of_rank_eq then gives rankAtStalk M p = n. Finite presentation is only used for the local-constancy step (Module.isLocallyConstant_rankAtStalk), and that step is not needed. hrank already forces the rank to be finite, so no finiteness of M is needed.

So for f = 0 the conclusion holds by `zero_mem _`, whatever `w.ord 0` and `h` say. Proof without `hf`: `by_cases hf : f = 0`. In the case f = 0, `subst`/rewrite and close with `zero_mem _`. In the case f ≠ 0, the existing proof runs unchanged; it is the only place `hf` is used, via `w.exists_unit_mul_zpow hf hπ`. ### [`P2MW.S_RubinSilverberg_exists_polynomial_rsFamily.M4cP2.D52.PhiA_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_RubinSilverberg_exists_polynomial_rsFamily.lean#L410-L414) — `flt-anthropic-0247`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0247.lean`

- `(hj : j < 21)` (theorem): `PhiPlusA j` and `PhiMinusA j` are the integer polynomial `sidePlusA`/`sideMinusA (hornerC naS X) (hornerC ngS X) (hornerC dtpS X) (hornerC a4zS X) (X + 600) t` with `t := (j : ℤ[X])` plugged in. So the identity holds for every `j`, not only for `j < 21`.

Argument: take `t` as a new variable `T` over the integral domain `ℤ[X]`. Let `G := sidePlusA (...) T - sideMinusA (...) T` in `ℤ[X][T]`.

**Degree of `G` in `T` is at most 20.**
- In `lhsPairAG`, `P5 = (na*T + …)^5` and `R = (ng*T + …)^5 * w^4` each have degree ≤ 5 in `T`. Every monomial `P5^a * R^b` has `a + b = 4`, so it has degree ≤ 20.
- The other part is `hornerG` of a list with one entry per row of `tabA`. `tabA` has exactly 21 rows, so this part also has degree ≤ 20 in `T`.

**`G` has 21 roots.** Evaluating `T := (j : ℤ[X])` turns `G` into `PhiPlusA j - PhiMinusA j`; this is what `map_sidePlusA`/`map_sideMinusA` give for the evaluation ring hom. The current theorem, which the existing proof establishes using `hj`, says this is 0 for `j = 0, …, 20`. These 21 values are distinct in the integral domain `ℤ[X]`.

**Conclusion.** `G` has degree ≤ 20 and 21 distinct roots, so `G = 0`. This is `eq_zero_of_natDegree_lt_card_of_eval_eq_zero`, the lemma the current proof already uses. Evaluating `G = 0` at `T = j` gives `PhiPlusA j = PhiMinusA j` for every natural `j`.

There are no degenerate cases to check: `j : ℕ` is unconstrained, and no junk values are involved.

**Proof without `hj`.** First prove the restricted statement exactly as now. Then prove the `T`-degree bound on `G` with `compute_degree`, after unfolding as in `natDegree_PhiA_le`. Finally apply the roots argument above.

The same reasoning works from the bivariate view: `F(s, t)` has degree ≤ 196 in `s` and ≤ 20 in `t` and vanishes on the 197×21 grid, so it is identically zero.

### [`P2MW.S_WeierstrassCurve_isTwoKernel_X_sub_C_coordsOrZero_of_addOrderOf_eq_two.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_WeierstrassCurve_isTwoKernel_X_sub_C_coordsOrZero_of_addOrderOf_eq_two.lean#L16-L30) — `flt-anthropic-0249`

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicGeometry_exists_restrict_action_quotient_clauses_of_isClopen_of_isConnected.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicGeometry_exists_restrict_action_quotient_clauses_of_isClopen_of_isConnected.lean#L229-L413) — `flt-anthropic-0266`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0266.lean`

- `[Finite G]` (theorem): The main proof uses finiteness only through the helper `range_ι_comp_eq_univ` (and `le_iSup_preimage_imagePreimage`, which calls it). Inside that helper the only use is `hWclosed : IsClosed W`, which applies `isClosed_iUnion_of_finite` to W = ⋃ g, (ρ g)⁻¹(U). That fact is only used to get h1 : IsClosed (π '' W). The proof itself shows hπW : π '' W = π '' U without any finiteness. So h1 also follows from `π.isClosedMap _ hUcl` (π is finite, hence universally closed), since U is closed. The rest of the clopen argument needs W to be open (any union of opens is) and its complement to be closed, which is true. Everything else in the main proof works for an arbitrary index type G: the `⨆ g : G` covers, the gluing `existsUnique_gluing'` and `eq_of_locally_eq'`. No other step needs `Fintype G`. So the statement stays true and provable for any group G.
- `(hsurj : Function.Surjective π.base)` (theorem): Surjectivity follows from `[IsFinite π]` and `hsec`. A finite morphism is universally closed, so `π.isClosedMap` holds and the range R = π '' univ is closed. Let V : X.Opens be the open complement of R. Then π ⁻¹ᵁ V = ⊥, so Γ(C, π ⁻¹ᵁ V) is terminal and hence subsingleton. The main proof already uses this fact: `CommRingCat.subsingleton_of_isTerminal (C.sheaf.isTerminalOfEqEmpty _)`. By `hsec V`, the map π.app V : Γ(X, V) → Γ(C, π ⁻¹ᵁ V) is injective, so Γ(X, V) is subsingleton too. If V contained a point, Mathlib's instance `Scheme.component_nontrivial` (Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/Properties.lean) would make Γ(X, V) nontrivial, which is a contradiction. So V is empty and π.base is surjective. The proof only uses hsurj by passing it to the helper lemmas, so this derived fact can be passed instead. This works with or without `[Finite G]`, so either hypothesis can be deleted on its own.

- `[hqN : Fact (¬ q ∣ N)]` (variable): The theorem takes this section variable automatically: it is instance-implicit, its type mentions only q and N, which are already arguments, and there is no `omit` for it in section L2. Nothing in the statement needs it. `PlaceSpecialization` requires only [Fact q.Prime] [NeZero N] [Field k] [CharP k q]. `PlaceSpecialization.fstDiv` (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_GlueData.lean) is `D.filter P.IsStrictFst`, and its variable block has no Fact (¬ q ∣ N). `modularFunctionFieldBar` takes only N. The claim is just that Finsupp.filter commutes with finite sums, which holds for any predicate. The proof uses only Finset.induction_on, Finset.sum_empty, Finsupp.filter_zero, Finset.sum_insert and the file's lemma `fstDiv_add`. The proof of `fstDiv_add` is `unfold fstDiv; exact Finsupp.filter_add` and never uses the divisibility fact. With `omit hqN in` on fstDiv_sum (and on fstDiv_add, or by calling Finsupp.filter_add directly), the same proof goes through.

### [`P2MW.S_GaloisRepAdic_isOrdinaryAt_of_forall_quotient.LCMazur.OrdQ.span_singleton_eq_range_ordProj`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_GaloisRepAdic_isOrdinaryAt_of_forall_quotient.lean#L263-L272) — `flt-anthropic-0275`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0275.lean`

- `(h2 : finrank A V = 2)` (theorem): `h2` follows from `hφ` and the other hypotheses. `[IsLocalRing A]` extends `Nontrivial A`, and a nontrivial commutative ring satisfies `StrongRankCondition` (Mathlib's `commRing_strongRankCondition` instance). Because `V` is free and finite, Mathlib's `LinearMap.charpoly_natDegree` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Charpoly/Basic.lean) gives `natDegree φ.charpoly = finrank A V`. By `hφ`, `φ.charpoly = (X - C α) * (X - C β)`. Each factor is monic of `natDegree` 1 (`monic_X_sub_C`, `natDegree_X_sub_C`; this uses `Nontrivial A`). So by `Monic.natDegree_mul` the product has `natDegree` 2, hence `finrank A V = 2`. The degenerate case where `A` is the zero ring cannot occur, since a local ring is nontrivial. So the proof can start with `have h2 : finrank A V = 2 := by rw [← LinearMap.charpoly_natDegree φ, hφ, (monic_X_sub_C α).natDegree_mul (monic_X_sub_C β), natDegree_X_sub_C, natDegree_X_sub_C]`, and the rest of the proof stays the same.

The proof without `ha` first splits on `by_cases ha : a = 0`. In the case a = 0, `subst` and then `simp`, or `iff_of_true` with the two facts above, closes the goal. - `[Algebra.IsSeparable K K']` (variable): The proof only uses separability to build `Field.powerBasisOfFiniteOfSeparable K K'`. Without the instance, the theorem can be proved by `by_cases h : Algebra.IsSeparable K K'`. If `h` holds, the existing proof works unchanged. If it fails, both sides are 0. On the right, `Algebra.trace_eq_zero_of_not_isSeparable` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Trace/Basic.lean:276; it needs no finiteness) gives `Algebra.trace K K' = 0`, so the right side is `algebraMap K F 0 = 0`. On the left, pick `θ : K'` with `¬ (minpoly K θ).Separable`. The file's own lemma `minpoly_algebraMap_eq_map` needs only `[FiniteDimensional K K']` and `hconst`, and gives `minpoly F (algebraMap K' F' θ) = (minpoly K θ).map (algebraMap K F)`. By `Polynomial.separable_map` (Mathlib/FieldTheory/Separable.lean:310) this polynomial is not separable either. So `algebraMap K' F' θ` is not separable over `F`, hence `¬ Algebra.IsSeparable F F'`, and `Algebra.trace_eq_zero_of_not_isSeparable` gives `Algebra.trace F F' = 0`. That makes the left side 0 too. There are no degenerate cases to worry about, since all four types are fields. The other hypotheses are needed: dropping `FiniteDimensional K K'` breaks the statement (K' = F = F' = K(t), where `trace` of `t` is `t` on one side and 0 on the other), and dropping either scalar-tower instance breaks it too (counterexample: all four fields equal to Q(√2) with K' → F' the conjugation map).

The hypothesis also follows from the others. In Mathlib's `RingTheory/Localization/FractionRing.lean` there is an instance (priority 100) `FaithfulSMul R K` derived from `[IsFractionRing R K]`, using `IsFractionRing.injective`. That instance needs no domain hypothesis. `K` is a field, so it is a domain. Then `IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K : IsDomain A` (in `Algebra/Algebra/Basic.lean`, with variables `[CommSemiring R] [Semiring A] [Algebra R A] [FaithfulSMul R A] [IsDomain A]`) gives the instance. The function `isDomain_iff_isField` in the same FractionRing file uses exactly this argument.

So the proof without the hypothesis starts with `haveI : IsDomain A := IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K` and then continues unchanged. The calls to `GenLift.exists_ringHom_forall_sub_mem` and the other helpers get their `[IsDomain A]` from this local instance.

The other hypotheses are genuinely needed:
- `IsAlgClosed K`: counterexample A = ℤ_p, H = A[X]/(X²−p), ψ sending X ↦ 0.
- `IsIntegrallyClosed A`: counterexample A = k + I inside a valuation ring with algebraically closed fraction field, H = A[X]/(X²−x²).
- `Module.Flat`: counterexample H = residue field.
- `IsLocalRing`: needed for the statement to elaborate.

This is used to show that `σ` has finite order. The other steps depend only on `L`: Haar uniqueness via `isMulLeftInvariant_eq_smul`, local compactness and second countability of the ideles of L, and the compact-neighbourhood argument showing `c = 1`. The earlier lemmas in this file also take `[NumberField K]` as an automatic instance argument but never use it in their proofs. The proof uses `ha` only for `Finset.card_image_of_injective` and `prod_image_eq_aeval_nodePoly`. Without it: let S := image a univ and apply `ModularCurve.exists_prod_mul_eq_aeval_of_forall_ord_nonneg_of_forall_neg_one_le_ord` with S and n = 0 as now. Its hypotheses come from hreg/hnode/hinf and do not need injectivity. This gives Q₀ with (Q₀ ≠ 0 → natDegree Q₀ ≤ S.card) and (∏_{s∈S}(jB - s)) * φ = aeval jB Q₀. ### [`P2MW.S_ModularForm_exists_gamma0_forall_tendsto_slash_atImInfty_of_three_le.A2EIS.tendsto_eisensteinSeries`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularForm_exists_gamma0_forall_tendsto_slash_atImInfty_of_three_le.lean#L183-L205) — `flt-anthropic-0329`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0329.lean`

- `[NeZero N]` (theorem): Nothing in the statement needs `NeZero N` to elaborate. In Mathlib, `gammaSet N r a` and `eisensteinSeries a k` (EisensteinSeries/Defs.lean) are defined for every `N`, and `cst` is too. The proof only uses `NeZero` in the step `ModularGroup_T_zpow_mem_verticalStrip z (NeZero.pos N)`, which moves a point into the vertical strip, and that step fails only when N = 0. So the theorem can be proved by splitting on whether N = 0. If N ≠ 0, we get the instance and the existing proof applies. If N = 0, then `ZMod 0 = ℤ` and the cast ℤ → ZMod 0 is the identity, so `gammaSet 0 1 a = {v | v = a ∧ gcd (v 0) (v 1) = 1}`. This set is `{a}` if a is coprime and empty otherwise. Since it has at most one element, `eisensteinSeries a k z` equals `eisSummand k a z` or `0`, and the limit can be checked case by case. (1) a is not coprime: the series is 0. Also a ≠ ![0,±1] because those vectors are coprime, so `cst k a = 0`. (2) a 0 ≠ 0 and a is coprime: the series is `eisSummand k a z`, which tends to `lim k a = 0` by the file's `tendsto_eisSummand hk` (it has no NeZero). Also a ≠ ![0,±1], so `cst k a = 0`. (3) a 0 = 0 and a is coprime: then |a 1| = 1, so a = ![0,1] (constant value 1 = cst) or a = ![0,-1] (constant value (-1)^(-k) = cst). In all three cases the limit is `cst k a`. Equivalently, for N = 0, take the finite sum over the subsingleton `gammaSet` of `tendsto_eisSummand`, and the resulting sum of `lim` values is `cst k a`; the proof of `tsum_lim` only uses `mem_gammaSet_iff_of_apply_zero`, which has no `NeZero`. So the theorem stays true and provable without `[NeZero N]`.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicGeometry_OModulePresheaf_exists_isCoherent_linearEquiv_of_forall_basicOpen_of_isBasis.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicGeometry_OModulePresheaf_exists_isCoherent_linearEquiv_of_forall_basicOpen_of_isBasis.lean#L1019-L1061) — `flt-anthropic-0333`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0333.lean`

- `(res_refl : ∀ (W : ↥B) (x : M W), res (le_refl W.1.1) x = x)` (theorem): `res_refl` follows from `res_comp` and `hqc` together. Fix `W : ↥B` and `x : M W`, and write `r := res (le_refl W.1.1)`. Any two proofs of `W.1.1 ≤ W.1.1` are equal by proof irrelevance, so every `res h` with `h : W.1.1 ≤ W.1.1` is the same map `r`.

(1) `r` is idempotent. Apply `res_comp` with `W = W' = W''` and `h = h' = le_refl _`. This gives `r x = r (r x)`, because `(le_refl _).trans (le_refl _)` is again a proof of `W ≤ W`.

(2) `r` is injective. Mathlib has `Scheme.basicOpen_one : X.basicOpen (1 : Γ(X, U)) = U` (Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/Scheme.lean:718). So `hqc W W 1 (V.basicOpen_one).symm` applies. Its second part says: if `r y = 0` then `∃ n, (1 : Γ(V, W.1.1)) ^ n • y = 0`, and `one_pow` and `one_smul` turn this into `y = 0`.

Take `y = r x - x`. By `map_sub` and (1), `r y = r (r x) - r x = 0`, so by (2) `y = 0`, i.e. `r x = x`. This is exactly `res_refl`.

The theorem's proof then changes in one place: build `D` with `res_refl := <the argument above>` and keep everything else. Nothing in the statement refers to `res_refl`, so it can be deleted without other changes.

The statement only involves `weight H μH`. `weight` (Definitions/Def_HaarQuotient.lean) needs only `[Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] [MeasurableSpace G]`, a subgroup and a measure. The proof is `measurable_weight` composed with the continuity of `y ↦ ↑y * g`. `measurable_weight` takes the same section instances, but its proof (`weight_eq`, then `Measurable.ennreal_tsum` and measurability of the indicators of open sets) never uses Haar properties of μH. So you can add `omit [μH.IsHaarMeasure] in` to both lemmas, or inline that short proof, and the result still holds. Measurability does not depend on which measure μH is.
- `[μH.IsMulRightInvariant]` (variable): Same reasoning as for `IsHaarMeasure`. The instance is pulled in automatically and never used. Neither `measurable_weight` nor the composition with the continuous map `y ↦ ↑y * g` needs any invariance of μH. Measurability is a property of the function alone, and it does not depend on μH.
- `[LocallyCompactSpace G]` (variable): `weight H μH` is defined as `if h : SigmaCompactSpace G ∧ WeaklyLocallyCompactSpace G then (tsum of constants times indicators of `interior (CompactExhaustion.choice G (n+1))`) else 0`. A proof that needs no local compactness: `unfold weight; split_ifs with h`. In the positive branch, take the instances from `h` and apply `Measurable.ennreal_tsum fun n => measurable_const.mul (measurable_const.indicator isOpen_interior.measurableSet)`. This needs only `BorelSpace G`, as in the existing `measurable_weight` proof. The negative branch is `measurable_const`. Then compose with `(continuous_subtype_val.mul continuous_const).measurable`, which uses `IsTopologicalGroup` and Borel measurability. So `weight` is measurable without `LocallyCompactSpace`, and the statement stays true and provable.
- `[SecondCountableTopology G]` (variable): The argument is the same as for `LocallyCompactSpace`. Second countability is only used, through instance synthesis, to obtain `SigmaCompactSpace G` and to choose the `dif_pos` branch in `weight_eq`. Splitting on the `dif` in the definition of `weight` instead gives measurability in both branches: the tsum of indicators of open sets with constant coefficients, or the constant 0. Neither branch needs second countability. The composition with the continuous map `y ↦ ↑y * g` is unchanged.

### [`GradedRing.Veronese.adjoin_component_one_eq_top`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/graded-rings/blob/05143eb425d31ab18c726dc431b2314834ab8094/GradedRings/VeroneseDegreeOne.lean#L80-L104) — `formal-frontier-0005`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0005.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): The statement is still true when n = 0. The library proves this case itself in `GradedRings/VeroneseZero.lean`, without `hgen` and without any finiteness assumption. There, `zeroVariable 𝒮 := DirectSum.of (fun j => 𝒮 (0 * j)) 1 (zeroUnit 𝒮 1)` lies in `component 𝒮 0 1`: `mem_component_iff` gives this directly, since `component` is defined as the image of `DirectSum.of _ j`. The theorem `aeval_zeroVariable_surjective` says that `Polynomial.aeval (R := component 𝒮 0 0) (zeroVariable 𝒮)` is surjective onto `VeroneseRing 𝒮 0`. It uses the same `Algebra (component 𝒮 n 0) (VeroneseRing 𝒮 n)` instance as this statement: the subring instance for the degree-zero part of the graded ring `component 𝒮 n` from `Veronese.lean`. A proof without `hn` therefore starts by case-splitting on n. If n = 0, take any x and write it as x = aeval (zeroVariable 𝒮) p. Then x ∈ adjoin {zeroVariable 𝒮} by Mathlib's `Polynomial.aeval_mem_adjoin_singleton`, and this is contained in `Algebra.adjoin (component 𝒮 0 0) (component 𝒮 0 1)` by `Algebra.adjoin_mono`, because zeroVariable ∈ component 𝒮 0 1. So the adjoin is ⊤. If n > 0, the existing proof applies unchanged. Degenerate cases: for the trivial ring S, every type involved is a subsingleton and the claim is immediate; the n = 0 argument above makes no nontriviality assumption anyway. Deleting `hn` does not affect how the statement elaborates, because `hn` appears nowhere else in it.

### [`Nat.DivisionGrowth.affine_lower_bound`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/sequence-growth/blob/7b7db3df8a043a09d037de4ff1eced6873404fb2/SequenceGrowth/Data/Nat/DivisionGrowth.lean#L68-L94) — `formal-frontier-0117`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0117.lean`

- `(hs : 0 < s)` (theorem): `hs` only excludes the case s = 0, and the statement already holds when s = 0. Then `(s - 1 : ℕ) = 0` by truncated subtraction, so offset = n * 0 / (n - 0) = 0. Also ratio = (n : ℚ) / 0 = 0, because division by zero is 0 in ℚ. The goal becomes `0 ^ m * (u 0 - 0) + 0 ≤ u m`. For m = 0 this is `u 0 ≤ u 0`. For m = i + 1 the left side is 0, and `0 ≤ (u m : ℚ)` holds since u m is a natural number cast to ℚ. Proof without `hs`: split on `s` with `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos s`. If s = 0, substitute and close the goal by `simp` plus `cases m` (`positivity` / `Nat.cast_nonneg` handles the successor case). If 0 < s, run the existing proof unchanged, using the new local hypothesis in place of `hs` (it is passed to `affine_step`). `hsn` is still needed: with s = n = 2, u 0 = 1 and u m = 0 for m ≥ 1, `hstep` holds but the claim at m = 1 is 1 ≤ 0, which is false.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.reducedIdentityComponent_topologicalKrullDim`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/GroupScheme/Dimension.lean#L265-L296) — `formal-frontier-0126`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0126.lean`

- `[IsAlgClosed K]` (variable): `[IsAlgClosed K]` is an argument because its type mentions `K`, which is an argument through `f`. The statement does not need it to elaborate. It is only used through `identityComponent_topologicalKrullDim`, whose proof uses `groupSchemePointComponent_surjective` (rational points meet every component). The equality also holds over any field, and the existing file's lemmas prove it.

Let H := Scheme.reductionOver (Over.mk f). The current proof already builds `GrpObj (Over.mk H.hom)` and `LocallyOfFiniteType H.hom` without IsAlgClosed: the reduction map is a closed immersion, composed with f. Let u := η[H].left (closedPoint K), and let p := (Scheme.identityComponentOne H).left (closedPoint K). Then `identityComponentι H` sends p to u, by `Scheme.identityComponentOne_comp_ι`.

(≤) `topologicalKrullDim (identityComponentOver H).left ≤ topologicalKrullDim H.left`, because the inclusion is an open embedding (`isOpenEmbedding.isInducing.topologicalKrullDim_le`, as in the existing proof).

(≥) Use `ringKrullDim_stalk_unit_eq_topologicalKrullDim K H.left H.hom` from this file. It needs only `Field K`, `GrpObj` and `LocallyOfFiniteType`, not IsAlgClosed. It gives `topologicalKrullDim H.left = ringKrullDim (stalk at u)`. By Mathlib's `ringKrullDim_stalk_eq_coheight` this equals `coheight u`. By `coheight_eq_of_isOpenImmersion` (the inclusion is an open immersion) this equals `coheight p`. Finally, `coheight p ≤ krullDim (IrreducibleCloseds _) = topologicalKrullDim (identityComponentOver H).left`, via `irreducibleSetEquivPoints` and `Order.coheight_le_krullDim`, exactly as in the first half of the proof of `ringKrullDim_stalk_unit_eq_topologicalKrullDim`.

The last step, `Scheme.reduction_topologicalKrullDim G`, does not involve K at all. Degenerate cases cause no trouble: Spec K is a nonempty one-point space, and the argument holds whether or not G is empty or of dimension ⊥.

### [`SpectralStoneDuality.chosenLimitCylinder_subset_iff_eventually`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/spectral-stone-duality/blob/78b803577679fc37120fa3406833af653fe12018/SpectralStoneDuality/LimitCylinderDescent.lean#L251-L261) — `formal-frontier-0141`

Mathlib provides `TopCat.topCat_hasLimitsOfSize [UnivLE.{v, u}] : HasLimitsOfSize.{w, v} TopCat.{u}` (Mathlib/Topology/Category/TopCat/Limits/Basic.lean:173). ### [`IsKrasner.adjoin_rootSet_le_adjoin_rootSet_of_norm_coeff_sub_lt`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/polynomial-root-stability/blob/ceae7822f6151ec6626678db1653054d3398cff3/PolynomialRootStability/Krasner.lean#L363-L391) — `formal-frontier-0152`

### [`MvPowerSeries.isUnit_adicallyRestricted_iff_coeff_radical`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/formal-power-series/blob/9bb57357300e4fad20d074ee4cd95e8317a34c81/FormalPowerSeries/MvPowerSeries/IdealAdicRestricted/UnitDetection.lean#L83-L92) — `formal-frontier-0162`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0162.lean`

- `[IsAdicComplete I R]` (variable): Nothing in the statement mentions `IsAdicComplete`. The theorem takes it only because it is an instance variable and `I` and `R` are arguments. The existing proof needs it twice: in `isUnit_adicallyRestricted_iff_adicReduction_one` (through the geometric-series inverse) and in `isUnit_iff_quotient_of_isAdicComplete`. With the RHS as stated (a unit constant coefficient in `R` itself, and the other coefficients in `I.radical`), completeness is not needed.

The definition `adicallyRestrictedSubring I` (IdealAdicRestricted.lean:78) says: for every k, the image of the series in (R/I^k)[[σ]] is a polynomial. It does not involve completeness.

(→) If f is a unit in the subring, then `coeff 0 = constantCoeff ∘ Subring.subtype` is a ring hom, so `coeff 0 f` is a unit. Also `adicReduction I 1 f` is a unit in `MvPolynomial σ (R ⧸ I^1)`. By Mathlib's `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff` (Algebra/MvPolynomial/Nilpotent.lean:58) together with `coeff_adicReduction` and `I^1 = I`, each `mk I (coeff α f)` with α ≠ 0 is nilpotent, i.e. `coeff α f ∈ I.radical`. This is exactly the first half of the existing proof of `..._coeff_mod_ideal`, and it does not use completeness.

(←) Since `coeff 0 f` is a unit, `MvPowerSeries.isUnit_iff_constantCoeff` gives an inverse g of f in `MvPowerSeries σ R`. It remains to show g is restricted. Fix k. The image of f in (R/I^k)[[σ]] is the coercion of a polynomial P_k. Its constant coefficient is a unit. Every other coefficient is the image of some r ∈ I.radical: if r^m ∈ I then r^{mk} ∈ I^k, so that image is nilpotent in R/I^k. By `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff`, P_k has an inverse Q_k in (R/I^k)[σ]. Inverses are unique in (R/I^k)[[σ]], so `map (mk (I^k)) g = ↑Q_k`, a polynomial. Hence g lies in the subring, and f is a unit there.

Degenerate cases are fine. If I = ⊤, every series is restricted and I.radical = ⊤, so both sides reduce to `coeff 0 f` being a unit. A trivial R and an empty σ are also harmless. So the theorem stays true and provable after `omit [IsAdicComplete I R] in`.

### [`FundamentalGroup.existsUnique_hom_of_isOpenCover`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/fundamental-groupoids/blob/2fdb99858e3feb7a6a06ae10915f286364de2cc1/FundamentalGroupoids/AlgebraicTopology/FundamentalGroup/OpenCover.lean#L404-L527) — `formal-frontier-0164`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0164.lean`

- `(hU : IsPathConnected (U : Set X))` (theorem): The current proof uses hU, through `leftArrow` and `globalArrow`. Even so, the statement still holds without it, given `hcover`, `hI` and the base point `p`. The other hypotheses do not need to change.

Let A be the path component of p in U and A' the path component of p in V. Since U ∩ V is path-connected (hI) and contains p, we have U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A', and therefore A ∩ A' = U ∩ V.

(1) The path component PC of p in X lies in A ∪ A'. Take a path γ from p. By hcover and a Lebesgue-number argument, γ splits into finitely many pieces, each lying in U or in V. Every junction between a U-piece and a V-piece lies in U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A'. By induction, each U-piece starts in A and so stays in A, and each V-piece stays in A'.

(2) From (1), U ∩ PC = A: a point of U ∩ PC lies in A or in A' ⊆ V, and in the second case it lies in U ∩ V ⊆ A. Likewise V ∩ PC = A'. So A and A' are open in PC and cover it, both are path-connected, and their intersection U ∩ V is path-connected.

(3) Every loop at p, and every homotopy of such loops, has path-connected image containing p. So the inclusions A ↪ U, A' ↪ V and PC ↪ X induce isomorphisms of fundamental groups at p.

Now apply the theorem itself, with all its hypotheses, to the space PC and the cover A, A'. Pull φU and φV back along the isomorphisms π₁(A,p) ≅ π₁(U,p) and π₁(A',p) ≅ π₁(V,p). The compatibility condition carries over, because the map U ∩ V → U factors through A. The theorem then gives a unique φ' : π₁(PC,p) →* G. Composing φ' with the inverse of π₁(PC,p) ≅ π₁(X,p) gives the required φ. Existence and uniqueness follow because the isomorphisms in (3) are bijective and every square commutes.

The degenerate cases are covered: `p` makes U ∩ V nonempty. Hence hU can be dropped. The same argument applies to hV, and in fact both can be dropped together.
- `(hV : IsPathConnected (V : Set X))` (theorem): This is symmetric to hU. Let A' be the path component of p in V (and A that of p in U). hI and `p ∈ U ∩ V` give U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A'. Splitting a path into pieces by a Lebesgue-number argument, using hcover, shows that the path component PC of p in X lies in A ∪ A'. It follows that V ∩ PC = A' and U ∩ PC = A, so A and A' are an open cover of PC by path-connected sets with path-connected intersection U ∩ V. The inclusions A ↪ U, A' ↪ V and PC ↪ X induce isomorphisms on π₁ at p, because loops and homotopies at p stay in the path component of p. So the universal property for X, U, V follows from the theorem (with all its hypotheses) applied to PC, A, A', by transporting along these isomorphisms. Thus hV can be deleted; hU and hV can even be deleted simultaneously.

### [`GradedRing.Veronese.finiteType_of_finiteType`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/graded-rings/blob/05143eb425d31ab18c726dc431b2314834ab8094/GradedRings/VeroneseFiniteType.lean#L105-L126) — `formal-frontier-0179`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0179.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): For n = 0, VeroneseRing 𝒮 0 = ⨁ j, 𝒮 (0*j). Each summand is 𝒮 0 and the multiplication is graded, so the ring is a copy of (𝒮 0)[t]. The base ring component 𝒮 0 0 is the image of DirectSum.of _ 0, and it acts through Mathlib's grade-zero algebra instance, whose algebraMap is the inclusion (SetLike.GradeZero.algebraMap_apply). Let t := DirectSum.of _ 1 ⟨1, one_mem_graded⟩. By DirectSum.of_mul_of, t^j = of j 1 and of j a = algebraMap (of 0 a') * t^j, where a' is a viewed in 𝒮 0. By DirectSum.induction_on, Algebra.adjoin (component 𝒮 0 0) {t} = ⊤, so Algebra.FiniteType follows from Subalgebra.fg_def with the finite set {t}. This case needs no hypothesis (not even FiniteType of S) and the trivial ring needs no special treatment. The revised proof splits on n: the argument above for n = 0, the existing proof for 0 < n.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.Proj.degreeScaledChartMap_preimage_basicOpen`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/projective-space/blob/3d39919aac0def769a0ff8b44e62a1317d4c84fe/ProjectiveSpace/DegreeScaledMap.lean#L453-L469) — `formal-frontier-0188`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0188.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): The degree-0 case `n = 0` follows from the positive-degree case. Write `s := (i.2 : A) ∈ 𝒜 i.1` with `0 < i.1`.
(1) Apply the theorem as stated to `r := s` (with `hr := i.2.2`, `hn := i.1.2`). This gives `chartMap ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen 𝒜 s = ι ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen ℬ (f s)`, and the right side is `⊤` because `ι` is the inclusion of `basicOpen ℬ (f s)`.
(2) For `r ∈ 𝒜 0`, `r * s ∈ 𝒜 (0 + i.1)` by `SetLike.GradedMul.mul_mem`, and that degree is positive. So the stated theorem applies to `r * s`. Mathlib's `Proj.basicOpen_mul` (ProjectiveSpectrum/Basic.lean) gives `basicOpen 𝒜 (r*s) = basicOpen 𝒜 r ⊓ basicOpen 𝒜 s`. Together with `map_mul`, it also gives `basicOpen ℬ (f r * f s) = basicOpen ℬ (f r) ⊓ basicOpen ℬ (f s)`. Preimages commute with `⊓`, and by (1) both `s`-factors pull back to `⊤`. This yields `chartMap ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen 𝒜 r = ι ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen ℬ (f r)`.
So the statement holds for every `n`, and the proof is the current one applied to `r*s` and `s`, plus `basicOpen_mul`. Pointwise, the chart map sends a homogeneous prime `q` to `f⁻¹(q)`, so the equality is `r ∉ f⁻¹ q ↔ f r ∉ q`, which holds in any degree.
- `(hr : r ∈ 𝒜 n)` (theorem): The equality also holds for non-homogeneous `r`. Mathlib's `ProjectiveSpectrum.basicOpen_eq_union_of_projection` (ProjectiveSpectrum/Topology.lean) gives `basicOpen 𝒜 r = ⨆ k, basicOpen 𝒜 (proj 𝒜 k r)`. Taking preimages commutes with `⨆`. Each component `r_k := proj 𝒜 k r` lies in `𝒜 k`, so the homogeneous version applies to it, using the degree-0 argument from the other entry when `k = 0`. This gives `ι ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen ℬ (f r_k)` for each `k`.
On the `B` side, `f r = Σ_k f r_k` with `f r_k ∈ ℬ (d*k)` (by `hdeg`), and `k ↦ d*k` is injective since `0 < d`. So `proj ℬ j (f r)` equals `f r_k` when `j = d*k` and `0` otherwise. Since `basicOpen ℬ 0 = ⊥`, the same lemma gives `basicOpen ℬ (f r) = ⨆ k, basicOpen ℬ (f r_k)`, and the two sides agree.
After the deletion, `n` and `hn` stay as harmless unused arguments, so the rest of the statement still elaborates. Pointwise, the chart map sends `q` to the homogeneous prime `f⁻¹ q`, and `r ∉ f⁻¹ q ↔ f r ∉ q` holds for every `r`.
`hr` and `hn` can each be deleted on their own, and also together.

### [`finiteGroupPresentation_iff_compact_totallyDisconnected`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/profinite-groups/blob/d21abb3f7fedd7241f3dbcb91ba8eed73d2caf9c/ProfiniteGroups/FiniteGroupCharacterization.lean#L198-L214) — `formal-frontier-0200`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0200.lean`

- `[T2Space G]` (theorem): The proof never uses it. Forward direction: CompactSpace and TotallyDisconnectedSpace are transported along the homeomorphism P.equiv.symm.toHomeomorph using Homeomorph.compactSpace and Homeomorph.totallyDisconnectedSpace, neither of which needs T2. Backward direction: `ProfiniteGrp.of` (Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Category/ProfiniteGrp/Basic.lean:81) only requires [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] [IsTopologicalGroup G] [CompactSpace G] [TotallyDisconnectedSpace G]. Its docstring says the Hausdorff condition can be omitted, because a totally disconnected group has {1} closed and is therefore Hausdorff. `FiniteGroupPresentation.ofProfiniteGrp` then gives the presentation. So the theorem and its proof go through unchanged without [T2Space G]. The trivial group needs no special treatment.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.ker_radical_of_isReduced_source`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/Scheme/JointlySchemeTheoreticallyDominant.lean#L154-L160) — `formal-frontier-0232`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0232.lean`

- `[QuasiCompact f]` (theorem): The proof uses `QuasiCompact f` only to rewrite with `Hom.ker_apply`, which turns `f.ker.ideal U` into `RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom`. The statement holds for any `f`. In Mathlib, `Hom.ker f := ofIdeals fun U ↦ RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom` (IdealSheaf/Basic.lean:691). Three facts give a proof without the hypothesis: `ofIdeals J` is the largest ideal sheaf whose ideals lie below `J` (`le_ofIdeals_iff : I ≤ ofIdeals J ↔ I.ideal ≤ J`), `ideal_ker_le` gives `f.ker.ideal U ≤ RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom` with no quasi-compactness, and `radical_ideal` gives `(I.radical).ideal U = (I.ideal U).radical`. Because X is reduced, each `RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom = comap (f.app U) ⊥` is a radical ideal (`Ideal.isRadical_bot.comap _`, as in the original proof). The order `≥` is `le_radical`. For `≤`, apply `le_ofIdeals_iff.mpr`. For each affine open U, `(f.ker.ideal U).radical ≤ (RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom).radical = RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom` follows from `Ideal.radical_mono (f.ideal_ker_le U)` and the radicality just shown. Then `le_antisymm` closes the goal. No step uses quasi-compactness. Degenerate case: if X is empty, then `f.ker = ⊤` and `⊤.radical = ⊤` (`radical_top`), which agrees with the general argument.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.iInf_ker_eq_vanishingIdeal_iSup_closure_range`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/Scheme/JointlySchemeTheoreticallyDominant.lean#L176-L185) — `formal-frontier-0233`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0233.lean`

- `[∀ j, QuasiCompact (f j)]` (theorem): The proof uses quasi-compactness only through the helper `Scheme.Hom.ker_eq_vanishingIdeal_closure_range`, which relies on Mathlib's `Hom.support_ker` (that lemma assumes `[QuasiCompact f]`). For a reduced source, the equality `f.ker = vanishingIdeal (closure (range f))` holds for any morphism. Mathlib defines `Hom.ker f := ofIdeals (fun U ↦ RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom)`, the largest ideal sheaf contained in the componentwise kernels. (1) Kernel ≤ vanishing ideal: Mathlib's `Hom.range_subset_ker_support` (no quasi-compactness assumption) gives `range f ⊆ f.ker.support`. The support is closed, so `closure (range f) ≤ f.ker.support`, and the Galois connection between support and vanishing ideal gives `f.ker ≤ vanishingIdeal (closure (range f))`. (2) Vanishing ideal ≤ kernel: by `le_ofIdeals_iff`, it suffices to show `(vanishingIdeal Z).ideal U ≤ RingHom.ker (f.app U)` for each affine open `U`. Let `s ∈ Γ(Y, U)` vanish at every point of `Z ∩ U`. Then `Y.basicOpen s` is disjoint from `range f`, so `X.basicOpen (f.app U s) = f ⁻¹ᵁ Y.basicOpen s = ⊥`. Since the source is reduced, `eq_zero_of_basicOpen_eq_bot` (Mathlib, `AlgebraicGeometry/Properties.lean`) gives `f.app U s = 0`. With this per-morphism equality, the rest of the proof (`vanishingIdeal_iSup`, `congr`, `funext`) is unchanged. Degenerate cases: if `J` is empty, both sides are `⊤`; if some `X j` is empty, its kernel is `⊤` and its closure of range is `⊥`, which agree. Reducedness, by contrast, is needed: for `X = Y = Spec k[ε]/(ε²)` and `f = 𝟙`, the kernel is `⊥` but the vanishing ideal of the whole space is the nilradical, which is not `⊥`.

### [`MvPowerSeries.existsUnique_firstVariable_division_of_scalar_top`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/formal-power-series/blob/9bb57357300e4fad20d074ee4cd95e8317a34c81/FormalPowerSeries/MvPowerSeries/IdealAdicRestricted/FirstVariableDivision.lean#L112-L132) — `formal-frontier-0285`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0285.lean`

- `(hprim : Ideal.span (Set.range (fun index : Fin (n + 1) →₀ ℕ => coeff index (f : MvPowerSeries (Fin (n + 1)) V))) = ⊤)` (theorem): `hprim` follows from `htop` and `hc`. Let x = coeff ((0 : Fin n →₀ ℕ).cons d) f. Mathlib's `MvPolynomial.finSuccEquiv_coeff_coeff` (Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Equiv.lean) with m = 0, rewritten using `htop`, gives (restrictedResidue a ham f).coeff ((0).cons d) = (MvPolynomial.C c).coeff 0 = c. By the library's `coeff_restrictedResidue`, that coefficient equals Ideal.Quotient.mk (maximalIdeal V) x. Since c ≠ 0, x is not in the maximal ideal, so `IsLocalRing.notMem_maximalIdeal` says x is a unit. x is in the range of the coefficient map, so the span contains a unit and equals ⊤ (`Ideal.eq_top_of_isUnit_mem`). The proof then goes through unchanged, using this derived fact wherever `hprim` was passed. This works independently of whether `ha` is also removed.
- `(ha : a ≠ 0)` (theorem): The case a = 0 is consistent with the other hypotheses, and the statement still holds there. If a = 0, then span{0} = ⊥, and its radical is the nilradical, which is ⊥ because V is a domain. So `hrad` forces maximalIdeal V = ⊥, which makes V a field and V ⧸ m ≅ V. `ham` holds trivially, and IsAdicComplete ⊥ V holds. In `adicallyRestrictedSubring ⊥` the k = 1 condition (V ⧸ ⊥ ≅ V) forces f to be a polynomial, so the restricted rings are exactly MvPolynomial (Fin (n+1)) V and MvPolynomial (Fin n) V. `polynomialRestrictedFinFirst` is the natural identification of V[X₁..Xₙ][X₀] with these polynomials (`coeff_polynomialRestrictedFinFirst`). `restrictedResidue f` is f itself. So `hdegree` and `htop` say that f, viewed as a polynomial in X₀ over V[X₁..Xₙ], has degree d and leading coefficient the nonzero constant c, which is a unit. Euclidean division by a polynomial with unit leading coefficient therefore gives h = q·f + P with deg P < d. The pair is unique: if q·f = P' − P with q ≠ 0, then deg(q·f) = deg q + d ≥ d (unit leading coefficient, domain), a contradiction. A proof without `ha` would split on `a = 0`: use the existing argument when a ≠ 0, and this polynomial-division argument when a = 0. That branch is substantial to write, because the current proof calls lemmas that take `ha`. `ha` and `hprim` can each be removed separately.

### [`IsFractionRing.isInteger_of_sq_mul_squarefree`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/quadratic-algebras/blob/85a6b1bc79989b524a4ecdae6cce17b712a7bd2d/QuadraticAlgebras/Squarefree.lean#L39-L62) — `formal-frontier-0311`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0311.lean`

- `[IsDomain A]` (variable): IsDomain A follows from [Field K] and [IsFractionRing A K]. Mathlib's RingTheory/Localization/FractionRing.lean (line 162) has the instance `FaithfulSMul R K`, built from `IsFractionRing.injective`; it needs only CommRing R, CommRing K, Algebra R K and IsFractionRing R K. `IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul` (Algebra/Algebra/Basic.lean, line 455) gives `IsDomain R` from `FaithfulSMul R A` and `IsDomain A`. A field is a domain, so `IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K : IsDomain A`; the underlying argument is `Function.Injective.isDomain` for the injective map `algebraMap A K`. A field is nontrivial, so A is nontrivial and there is no degenerate case. The statement itself (Squarefree f, algebraMap, IsLocalization.IsInteger) does not need IsDomain A to elaborate; only the proof's lemmas (num/den, num_den_reduced, isInteger_of_isUnit_den) use it. So: add `omit [IsDomain A] in` before the theorem, start the proof with `haveI : IsDomain A := IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K`, and leave the rest of the proof unchanged. These lemmas were checked in current Mathlib master, and the change was not compiled.

### [`Matrix.normalizedRowRank_mul_le_left`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/stable-range/blob/c283b01ed9ebc33c7eefcdb4f3d2ca45141f469e/StableRange/DivisionRingRank.lean#L397-L400) — `formal-frontier-0353`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0353.lean`

- `[Nonempty n]` (variable): normalizedRowRank A is defined as (rowRank A : ℝ) / Fintype.card n, so both sides share the same denominator. The proof uses Nonempty only through card_cast_pos (0 < card n) to apply div_le_div_iff_of_pos_right. Mathlib's `div_le_div_of_nonneg_right (hab : a ≤ b) (hc : 0 ≤ c) : a / c ≤ b / c` (Mathlib/Algebra/Order/GroupWithZero/Basic.lean) needs only 0 ≤ (card n : ℝ), which is Nat.cast_nonneg _. So the proof becomes `div_le_div_of_nonneg_right (by exact_mod_cast rowRank_mul_le_left A B) (Nat.cast_nonneg _)`, and rowRank_mul_le_left does not need Nonempty. Degenerate case: if n is empty, card n = 0 and x / 0 = 0 makes both sides 0, so the statement still holds.
